**Virginia**, officially the **Commonwealth of Virginia**,[^cite_note-5] is a [state](18618239.md) in the [Southeastern](816925.md) and [Mid-Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Atlantic_\(United_States\)) regions of the United States between the [Atlantic Coast](89126.md) and the [Appalachian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_Mountains). It borders [Kentucky](16846.md) to the west, [Tennessee](30395.md) to the south-west, [North Carolina](21650.md) to the south, [West Virginia](32905.md) to the north-west, and [Maryland](18858.md) to the north. The state's [capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States) is [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia) and its most populous city is [Virginia Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach,_Virginia). With a population of 8.8 million, it is the [12th-most populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population) and [15th-most densely populated state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population_density). More than one-third of Virginia's population lives in [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia), which includes the most populous jurisdiction in the state, [Fairfax County](91327.md).

Eastern Virginia is part of the [Atlantic Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Plain), and the [Middle Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Peninsula) forms the mouth of the [Chesapeake Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay). Central Virginia lies predominantly in the [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont_\(plateau\)), the foothill region of the [Blue Ridge Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge_Mountains), which cross the western and southwestern parts of the state. The fertile [Shenandoah Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_Valley) fosters the state's most productive agricultural counties, while the economy in Northern Virginia is driven by [technology companies](44211754.md) and [U.S. federal government](195149.md) agencies. [Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads) is also the site of the [region's main seaport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Port_Authority) and [Naval Station Norfolk](896196.md), the world's largest naval base.

Virginian history begins with [several Indigenous nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_tribes_in_Virginia), some of which were members of the [Powhatan Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powhatan_Confederacy). In 1607, the [London Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Company) established the [Colony of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Virginia) as the first permanent [English colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_overseas_possessions) in the [New World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_World), leading to Virginia's nickname as **the Old Dominion**. Slaves from Africa and land from displaced native tribes fueled the growing plantation economy, but also fueled conflicts both inside and outside the colony. Virginians [fought for the independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_in_the_American_Revolution) of the [Thirteen Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies) in the [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution), and helped establish the new national government. During the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_in_the_American_Civil_War), the state government in Richmond joined the [Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederated_States_of_America), while many northwestern counties remained loyal to the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(American_Civil_War\)), which led to the separation of [West Virginia](32905.md) in 1863.

Although the state was under [one-party rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solid_South) for nearly a century following the [Reconstruction era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_era_of_the_United_States), both major political parties have been competitive in Virginia since the repeal of [racial segregation laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws) in the 1960s and 1970s. Virginia's state legislature is the [Virginia General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_Assembly), which was established in July 1619, making it the oldest extant law-making body in [North America](21139.md). Unlike other states, cities and counties in Virginia function as approximate equals, but the state government manages local roads in most counties. It is also the only state where governors are prohibited from serving consecutive terms.

## History

Main article: [History of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Virginia)

### Earliest inhabitants

Main article: [Native American tribes in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_tribes_in_Virginia)

A simple drawing of a young dark-haired Native American woman speaking to two men in armor from the early 1600s. Several Native Americans look on from the right.

The story of [Pocahontas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pocahontas) was simplified and romanticized by later artists and authors, including [Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Smith_\(explorer\)) himself, and promoted by her descendants, some of whom married into [elite colonial families](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Families_of_Virginia).

[^cite_note-slate_pocahontas-6]

Hunter-gatherers are [estimated to have arrived](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peopling_of_the_Americas) in Virginia around 17,000 years ago. Evidence from [Daugherty's Cave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daugherty's_Cave_and_Breeding_Site) shows it was regularly used as a [rock shelter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_shelter) by 9,800 years ago.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEgloffWoodward20062–14-7] During the late [Woodland period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodland_period) (500–1000 AD), farming spread to the region, first corn and squash, then later beans and tobacco arriving from [the southwest](179571.md) and Mexico by the end of the period. [Palisaded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palisade) towns began to be built around 1200. The native population in the current boundaries of Virginia reached around 50,000 in the 1500s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEgloffWoodward20065,_31–39-8] Large nations in the area at that time included the tribes of Tsenacommacah such as the [Chesapeake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_people), [Pamunkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamunkey) and [Chickahominy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chickahominy_people) along the coastline. To their south was the [Nottoway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nottoway_Tribe) and [Meherrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meherrin) with the [Tutelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutelo) further inland to the west.[^cite_note-heinemann-9]

In response to threats from these other groups to their trade network, 30 or so [Tsenacommacan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsenacommacan) tribes consolidated under Wahunsenacawh (commonly called [Chief Powhatan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Powhatan)) starting in the 1570s.[^cite_note-heinemann-9] The [Powhatan Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powhatan_Confederacy) governed more than 150 settlements that had a total population of around 15,000 in 1607.[^cite_note-10] Three-fourths of the native population in Virginia, however, died from [smallpox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smallpox) and other [Old World diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_World_diseases) during that century,[^cite_note-11] disrupting their [oral traditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oral_tradition) and complicating research into earlier periods.[^cite_note-12] Additionally, many primary sources, including those that mention Powhatan's daughter, [Pocahontas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pocahontas), were created by [Europeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_people), who may have held biases or misunderstood native social structures and customs.[^cite_note-slate_pocahontas-6][^cite_note-13]

### Colony

Main article: [Colony of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Virginia)

Several European expeditions, including a [group of Spanish Jesuits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajac%C3%A1n_Mission), explored the [Chesapeake Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay) during the 16th century.[^cite_note-14] To help counter [Spain's colonies in the Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_West_Indies), Queen [Elizabeth I of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_I_of_England) supported [Walter Raleigh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Raleigh)'s 1584 expedition to the [Atlantic coast of North America](89126.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallenstein20078–9-15][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoran20078-16] The name "Virginia" was used by Captain [Arthur Barlowe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Barlowe) in the expedition's report, and may have been suggested by Raleigh or Elizabeth (perhaps noting her status as the "Virgin Queen" or that they viewed the land as being untouched) or related to an [Algonquin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquin_language) phrase, *Wingandacoa* or *Windgancon*, or leader's name, [Wingina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wingina), as heard by the expedition.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStewart200822-17][^cite_note-18] The name initially applied to the entire coastal region from [South Carolina](27956.md) in the south to [Maine](19977.md) in the north, along with the island of [Bermuda](3460.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVollmann2002695–696-19] [Raleigh's colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke_Colony) failed, but the potential financial and strategic gains still captivated many English policymakers. In 1606, [King James I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_I_of_England) issued a [charter for a new colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Virginia_Charter) to the [Virginia Company of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Company). The group financed an expedition under [Christopher Newport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Newport) that established a settlement named [Jamestown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown,_Virginia) in 1607.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEConlin200930–31-20]

Though more settlers soon joined, many were ill-prepared for the dangers of the new settlement. As the colony's president, [John Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Smith_\(explorer\)) secured food for the colonists from nearby tribes, but after he left in 1609, this trade stopped and a [series of ambush-style killings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Powhatan_Wars) between colonists and natives under [Chief Powhatan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powhatan_\(Native_American_leader\)) and [his brother](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opechancanough) began, resulting in [mass starvation in the colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starving_Time_\(Jamestown\)) that winter.[^cite_note-21] By the end of the colony's first 14 years, over 80% of the roughly eight thousand settlers transported there had died.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade200730-22] [Demand for exported tobacco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tobacco_in_the_American_Colonies), however, fueled the need for more workers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallenstein200722-23] Starting in 1618, the [headright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Headright) system tried to solve this by granting colonists farmland for their help attracting [indentured servants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indentured_servant).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHashaw200776–77,_239–240-24] Enslaved Africans [were first sold in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Africans_in_Virginia) in 1619. Though other Africans arrived as indentured servants and could be freed after four to seven years, the basis for [lifelong slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_slavery_in_Virginia) was developed in legal cases like those of [John Punch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Punch_\(slave\)) in 1640 and [John Casor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Casor) in 1655.[^cite_note-25] Laws passed in Jamestown defined slavery as [race-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_\(human_categorization\)) in 1661, as [inherited maternally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partus_sequitur_ventrem) in 1662, and as enforceable by death in 1669.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHashaw2007211–215-26]

A three-story red brick colonial-style hall and its left and right wings during summer.

In 1699, after the statehouse in [Jamestown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown,_Virginia) was destroyed by fire, the [Colony of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Virginia)'s capitol was moved to [Williamsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williamsburg,_Virginia), where the [College of William & Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_William_&_Mary) was founded six years earlier.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade200776–77-27]

From the colony's start, residents agitated for greater local control, and in 1619, certain male colonists began electing representatives to an assembly, later called the [House of Burgesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Burgesses), that negotiated issues with the [governing council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Governor's_Council) appointed by the London Company.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordon200417-28] Unhappy with this arrangement, the monarchy revoked the company's charter and began directly naming [governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colonial_governors_of_Virginia) and Council members in 1624. In 1635, colonists arrested [a governor who ignored the assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Harvey_\(Virginia_governor\)) and sent him back to England against his will.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade200732,_37-29] [William Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Berkeley_\(governor\)) was named governor in 1642, just as the turmoil of the [English Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Civil_War) and [Interregnum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Interregnum) permitted the colony greater autonomy.[^cite_note-30] As a supporter of the king, Berkeley welcomed other [Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Cavaliers_\(historical\)) who fled to Virginia. He surrendered to [Parliamentarians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roundhead) in 1652, but after the 1660 [Restoration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuart_Restoration) made him governor again, he blocked assembly elections and exacerbated the [class divide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Class_stratification) by disenfranchising and restricting the movement of indentured servants, who made up around 80 percent of the workforce.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETarter202062-31] On the colony's frontier, [tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_tribes_in_Virginia) like the [Tutelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutelo) and [Doeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doeg_people) were being squeezed by [Seneca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seneca_people) raiders from the north, leading to more confrontations with colonists. In 1676, several hundred working-class followers of [Nathaniel Bacon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathaniel_Bacon_\(Virginia_colonist\)), upset by Berkeley's refusal to retaliate against the tribes, burned Jamestown.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade200751–59-32]

[Bacon's Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bacon's_Rebellion) forced the signing of [Bacon's Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bacon's_Laws), which restored some of the colony's rights and sanctioned both attacks on native tribes and the enslavement of their people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETarter202051–57-33][^cite_note-34] The [Treaty of 1677](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_1677) further reduced the independence of the tribes that signed it, and aided the colony's assimilation of their land in the years that followed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade200757-35][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShefveland201659–62-36] Colonists in the 1700s were pushing westward into the area held by the Seneca and their larger [Iroquois Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iroquois), and in 1748, a group of wealthy speculators, backed by the British monarchy, formed the [Ohio Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Company) to start English settlement and trade in the [Ohio Country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Country) west of the [Appalachian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_Mountains).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnderson200023-37] France, which claimed this area as part of [New France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_France), viewed this as a threat, and in 1754 the [French and Indian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_and_Indian_War) engulfed England, France, the Iroquois, and other allied tribes on both sides. A militia from several British colonies, called the [Virginia Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Regiment), was led by Major [George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington), himself one of the investors in the Ohio Company.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnderson200042–43-38]

### Statehood

See also: [Virginia in the American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_in_the_American_Revolution)

Upper-class middle-aged man dressed in a bright red cloak speaks before an assembly of other angry men. The subject's right hand is raise high in gesture toward the balcony.

In 1765, [Patrick Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Henry) led a [protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Resolves) of the unpopular [Stamp Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stamp_Act_1765) in the [House of Burgesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Burgesses), later depicted in this portrait by [Peter F. Rothermel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_F._Rothermel).

In the decade following the [French and Indian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_and_Indian_War), the [British Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Great_Britain) passed new taxes which were deeply unpopular in the colonies. In the [House of Burgesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Burgesses), opposition to [taxation without representation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_taxation_without_representation) was led by [Patrick Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Henry) and [Richard Henry Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Henry_Lee), among others.[^cite_note-39] Virginians began to [coordinate their actions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_correspondence) with other colonies in 1773 and sent delegates to the [Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Congress) the following year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGutzman200724–29-40] After the House of Burgesses was dissolved in 1774 by [the royal governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Murray,_4th_Earl_of_Dunmore), Virginia's revolutionary leaders continued to govern via the [Virginia Conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Conventions). On May 15, 1776, the Convention declared Virginia's independence and adopted [George Mason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Mason)'s [Virginia Declaration of Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Declaration_of_Rights), which was then included in a new constitution that designated Virginia as a [commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade2007125–133-41] Another Virginian, [Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson), drew upon Mason's work in drafting the national [Declaration of Independence](31874.md).[^cite_note-mason-42]

After the [American Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War) began, [George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington) was selected by the [Second Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Continental_Congress) in [Philadelphia](50585.md) to head the [Continental Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Army), and many [Virginians joined the army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Line) and revolutionary militias. Virginia was the first colony to ratify the [Articles of Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articles_of_Confederation) in December 1777.[^cite_note-43] In April 1780, the capital was moved to [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia) at the urging of Governor Thomas Jefferson, who feared that Williamsburg's coastal location would make it vulnerable to British attack.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECooper200758-44] In January 1781, [Benedict Arnold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedict_Arnold)'s British forces [raided Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raid_on_Richmond) before establishing a base at [Portsmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portsmouth,_Virginia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKetchum2014155-45] The British army had over seven thousand soldiers and twenty-five warships stationed in Virginia at the beginning of 1781, but [General Charles Cornwallis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Cornwallis,_1st_Marquess_Cornwallis) and his superiors were indecisive, and maneuvers by the three thousand soldiers under the [Marquis de Lafayette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquis_de_Lafayette) and twenty-nine allied French warships together managed to [confine the British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yorktown_campaign) to a swampy area of the [Virginia Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Peninsula) in September. Around 16,000 soldiers under George Washington and [Comte de Rochambeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comte_de_Rochambeau) quickly [converged there](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington%E2%80%93Rochambeau_Revolutionary_Route) and defeated Cornwallis in the [siege of Yorktown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Yorktown).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKetchum2014126–131,_137–139,_296-46] His surrender on October 19, 1781, led to [peace negotiations in Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_of_Paris_\(1783\)) and secured the independence of the colonies.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade2007131–133-47]

Virginians were instrumental in writing the [United States Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Constitution). [James Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Madison) drafted the [Virginia Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Plan) in 1787 and the [Bill of Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Bill_of_Rights) in 1789,[^cite_note-mason-42] and [Virginia ratified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Ratifying_Convention) the Constitution on June 25, 1788. The [three-fifths compromise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three-fifths_compromise) ensured that Virginia, with its large number of slaves, initially had the largest bloc in the [House of Representatives](19468510.md). Together with the [Virginia dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_dynasty) of presidents, this gave the Commonwealth national importance. Virginia is called the "Mother of States" because of its role in being carved into states such as [Kentucky](16846.md), and for the numbers of [American pioneers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_pioneer) born in Virginia.[^cite_note-Robertson-48]

### Civil War

Main article: [Virginia in the American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_in_the_American_Civil_War)

A family of eight women and children sit on a bench behind a cylindrical metal heater, while one adult male sits on his own to the right.

[Eyre Crowe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyre_Crowe_\(painter\))'s 1853 portrait, *Slaves Waiting for Sale: Richmond, Virginia*, which he completed after visiting [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia)'s slave markets, where thousands were sold annually

[^cite_note-49]

Between 1790 and 1860, the number of [slaves in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_slavery_in_Virginia) rose from around 290,000 to over 490,000, roughly one-third of the state population, and the number of slave owners rose to over 50,000. Both of these numbers represented the most in the U.S.[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorgan1998490-51] The boom in [Southern cotton production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton_Belt) using [cotton gins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton_gin) to harvest [upland cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gossypium_hirsutum) increased the amount of labor needed, but [new federal laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_Prohibiting_Importation_of_Slaves) prohibited the importation of slaves. Decades of [monoculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoculture) tobacco farming had also [degraded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_degradation) Virginia's [agricultural productivity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_productivity).[^cite_note-fischer-52] Virginia plantations increasingly turned to [exporting slaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_trade_in_the_United_States), which broke up countless families and made the [breeding of slaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_breeding_in_the_United_States), often through rape, a profitable business.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryson2011466–467-53][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJordan1995119–122-54] Slaves in the [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia) area were also forced into industrial jobs, including mining and shipbuilding.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis2006125,_208–210-55] The failed slave uprisings of [Gabriel Prosser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Prosser) in 1800, [George Boxley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Boxley) in 1815, and [Nat Turner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nat_Turner) in 1831, however, marked the growing resistance to slavery. Afraid of further uprisings, Virginia's government in the 1830s encouraged [Free Blacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Blacks) to migrate to [Liberia](17791.md).[^cite_note-fischer-52]

On October 16, 1859, abolitionist [John Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Brown_\(abolitionist\)) led a [raid on Harpers Ferry, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Brown's_Raid_on_Harpers_Ferry), in an attempt to start a slave revolt across the southern states. The polarized national response to his raid, capture, trial, and execution that December marked a tipping point for many who believed slavery would need to be ended by force.[^cite_note-56] [Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln)'s 1860 election further convinced many southern supporters of slavery that his opposition to its expansion would ultimately mean the end of slavery across the country. The [seizure of Fort Sumter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Fort_Sumter) by [Confederate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America) forces on April 14, 1861, prompted Lincoln to [call for the federalization of 75,000 militiamen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_80).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJaffa2000230–236,_357–358-57]

A color drawing of a city skyline in flames as a steady stream of people on horses or in horse-drawn carriages cross a long bridge over a river.

The [Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America) used [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia) as their capital from May 1861 till April 1865, when they abandoned the city and set fire to [its downtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shockoe_Slip).

The [Virginia Secession Convention of 1861](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Secession_Convention_of_1861) voted on April 17 [to secede](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinance_of_Secession) on the condition it was approved in a referendum the next month. The convention voted to join the Confederacy, which named [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia) its capital on May 20.[^cite_note-Robertson-48] During the May 23 referendum, armed pro-Confederate groups prevented the casting and counting of votes from areas that opposed secession. Representatives from 27 of these northwestern counties instead began the [Wheeling Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheeling_Convention), which organized a government loyal to the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(Civil_War\)) and led to the separation of [West Virginia](32905.md) as a new state.[^cite_note-58]

The armies of the Union and Confederacy first met on July 21, 1861, at [Bull Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Battle_of_Bull_Run) near [Manassas, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manassas,_Virginia), a bloody Confederate victory. Union General [George B. McClellan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_B._McClellan) organized the [Army of the Potomac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_the_Potomac), which [landed on the Virginia Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula_campaign) in March 1862 and reached the outskirts of Richmond that June. With Confederate General [Joseph E. Johnston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_E._Johnston) wounded in fighting outside the city, command of his [Army of Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_Northern_Virginia) fell to [Robert E. Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_E._Lee). Over the next month, Lee [drove the Union army back](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Days_Battles), and starting that September led [the first of several invasions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maryland_campaign) into Union territory. During the next three years of war, more battles were fought in Virginia than anywhere else, including the battles of [Fredericksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Fredericksburg), [Chancellorsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Chancellorsville), [Spotsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Spotsylvania_Court_House), and the concluding [Battle of Appomattox Court House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Appomattox_Court_House), where Lee surrendered on April 9, 1865.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodwin20124-59]

### Reconstruction and segregation

Several World War I ships line a port crowded with warehouses, with a city skyline behind them.

With nearly 800,000 American soldiers passing through its terminals, the [Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads) Port of Embarkation at Newport News became the second-largest U.S. port of embarkation during [World War I](4764461.md).

[^cite_note-embarkation-60]

Virginia was formally restored to the United States in 1870, due to the work of the [Committee of Nine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_Nine).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade2007249–250-61] During the post-war [Reconstruction era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_era_of_the_United_States), African Americans were able to unite in communities, particularly around [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia), [Danville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danville,_Virginia), and the [Tidewater region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidewater_\(region\)), and take a greater role in Virginia society; many achieved some land ownership during the 1870s.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis2006328–329-63] Virginia [adopted a constitution in 1868](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Constitutional_Convention_of_1868) which guaranteed political, civil, and [voting rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_rights_in_the_United_States), and provided for free public schools.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorgan1992160–166-64] However, with many railroad lines and other infrastructure destroyed during the Civil War, the Commonwealth was deeply in debt, and in the late 1870s redirected money from public schools to pay bondholders. The [Readjuster Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Readjuster_Party) formed in 1877 and won legislative power in 1879 by uniting Virginians across racial lines behind a shared opposition to debt payments and the perceived [plantation elites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planter_class).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaileyGilmoreSimon200090–96-65]

The Readjusters focused on building up schools, like [Virginia Tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Tech) and [Virginia State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_State_University), and successfully forced [West Virginia](32905.md) to share in the pre-war debt.[^cite_note-66] But in 1883, they were divided by a proposed repeal of [anti-miscegenation laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-miscegenation_laws), and days before that year's election, a [riot in Danville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danville_Massacre), involving armed policemen, left five men dead, four of whom were Black.[^cite_note-67] These events motivated a push by [white supremacists](41154041.md) to seize political power through [voter suppression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_suppression_in_the_United_States), and segregationists in the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Virginia) won the legislature that year and [maintained control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solid_South) for decades.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaileyGilmoreSimon200099–103-68] They passed [Jim Crow laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws) that established a [racially segregated society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation_in_the_United_States), and in 1902 rewrote the [state constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Virginia) to include a [poll tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poll_tax_\(United_States\)) and other voter registration measures that effectively [disenfranchised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disfranchisement_after_the_Reconstruction_era) most African-American and lower-income Virginians.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallenstein2007253–254-69]

New economic forces meanwhile industrialized the Commonwealth. Virginian [James Albert Bonsack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Albert_Bonsack) invented the tobacco cigarette rolling machine in 1880 leading to new large-scale production centered around Richmond. Railroad magnate [Collis Potter Huntington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collis_Potter_Huntington) founded [Newport News Shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport_News_Shipbuilding) in 1886, which was responsible for building 38 warships for the [U.S. Navy](20518076.md) between 1907 and 1923.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStyron201142–43-70] During [World War I](4764461.md), German submarines attacked ships outside the port,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFeuer199950–52-71] which was a major site for transportation of soldiers and supplies.[^cite_note-embarkation-60] After the war, a homecoming parade to honor African-American troops was [attacked in July 1919](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1919_Norfolk_race_riot) by the city's police as part of a renewed white supremacy movement, known as [Red Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Summer).[^cite_note-72] The shipyard continued building warships in [World War II](32927.md), and quadrupled its pre-war labor force to 70,000 by 1943. The [Radford Arsenal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radford_Army_Ammunition_Plant) outside [Blacksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacksburg,_Virginia) also employed 22,000 workers making explosives,[^cite_note-73] while the [Torpedo Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torpedo_Factory_Art_Center) in [Alexandria](18940583.md) had over 5,050.[^cite_note-74]

### Civil rights to present

A bronze statue of a man riding a horse on a tall pedestal that is covered in colorful graffiti.

Protests in 2020 focused on [Confederate monuments in the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Confederate_monuments_and_memorials_in_Virginia).

High-school student [Barbara Rose Johns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Rose_Johns) started a strike in 1951 at her underfunded and segregated school in [Prince Edward County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Edward_County,_Virginia). The protests led [Spottswood Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spottswood_William_Robinson_III) and [Oliver Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Hill_\(attorney\)) to file [a lawsuit against the county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_v._County_School_Board_of_Prince_Edward_County). Their case joined *[Brown v. Board of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_v._Board_of_Education)* at the Supreme Court, which rejected the doctrine of "[separate but equal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separate_but_equal)" in 1954. The segregationist establishment, led by Senator [Harry F. Byrd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_F._Byrd) and his [Byrd Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byrd_Organization), reacted with a strategy called "[massive resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massive_resistance)", and the General Assembly passed [a package of laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Plan) in 1956 that cut off funding to local schools that [desegregated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desegregation_in_the_United_States), causing some to close. Courts ruled the strategy unconstitutional, and on February 2, 1959, Black students [integrated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_integration) schools in [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) and [Norfolk](57898.md), where they were known as the [Norfolk 17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_17).[^cite_note-75] Rather than integrate, county leaders in Prince Edward shut their school system in June 1959. When [litigation again reached the Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griffin_v._County_School_Board_of_Prince_Edward_County), it ordered the county to reopen and integrate its schools, which finally happened in September 1964.[^cite_note-76][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallenstein2007340–341,_350–357-77]

Federal passage of the [Civil Rights Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights_Act_of_1964) (1964) and [Voting Rights Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_Rights_Act_of_1965) (1965), and their later enforcement by the [Justice Department](52563.md), helped end racial segregation in Virginia and overturn [Jim Crow laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws).[^cite_note-78] In 1967, the Supreme Court struck down the state's ban on [interracial marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interracial_marriage_in_the_United_States) with *[Loving v. Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loving_v._Virginia)*. In 1968, Governor [Mills Godwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mills_Godwin) called a commission to rewrite the state constitution. The new constitution, which banned discrimination and removed articles that now violated federal law, [passed in a referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_Virginia_ballot_measures) and went into effect in 1971.[^cite_note-constitution_1970-79] In 1989, [Douglas Wilder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Wilder) became the first African American elected as governor in the United States, and in 1992, [Bobby Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Scott_\(politician\)) became the first Black congressman from Virginia since 1888.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinemannKolpParentShade2007359–366-80][^cite_note-81]

The expansion of federal government offices into Northern Virginia's suburbs during the [Cold War](325329.md) boosted the region's population and economy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAccordino200076–78-82] The [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) outgrew their offices in [Foggy Bottom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foggy_Bottom) during the [Korean War](16772.md), and moved to [Langley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langley,_Virginia) in 1961, in part due to a decision by the [National Security Council](44026.md) that the agency relocate outside the District of Columbia.[^cite_note-83] [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon), built in [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) during [World War II](32927.md) as the headquarters of the Department of Defense, was struck by a hijacked plane in the [September 11, 2001 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11,_2001_attacks).[^cite_note-84] Mass shootings at [Virginia Tech in 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Tech_shooting) and in [Virginia Beach in 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Virginia_Beach_shooting) led to passage of gun control measures in 2020.[^cite_note-85] Racial injustice and the presence of [Confederate monuments in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Confederate_monuments_and_memorials_in_Virginia) have also led to large demonstrations, including in August 2017, when a white supremacist [drove his car into protesters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville_car_attack), killing one, and in June 2020, when protests that were part of the larger [Black Lives Matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Lives_Matter) movement brought about the [removal of Confederate statues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Removal_of_Confederate_monuments_and_memorials#Virginia).[^cite_note-86]

## Geography

Main article: [Environment of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_Virginia)

A topographic map of Virginia, with text identifying cities and natural features.

Virginia is shaped by the [Blue Ridge Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge_Mountains), the [Chesapeake Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay) and its watershed, and the [parallel 36°30′ north](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_36%C2%B030%E2%80%B2_north).

Virginia is located in the [Mid-Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Atlantic_\(United_States\)) and [Southeastern](816925.md) regions of the United States.[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88] Virginia has a total area of 42,774.2 square miles (110,784.7 km<sup>2</sup>), including 3,180.13 square miles (8,236.5 km<sup>2</sup>) of water, making it the 35th-[largest state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_area) by area.[^cite_note-89] It is bordered by [Maryland](18858.md) and [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) to the northeast; by the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md) to the east; by [North Carolina](21650.md) to the south; by [Tennessee](30395.md) to the southwest; by [Kentucky](16846.md) to the west; and by [West Virginia](32905.md) to the northwest. Virginia's boundary with Maryland and Washington, D.C., the low-water mark of the south shore of the [Potomac River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potomac_River), has been an issue for [water rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_right).[^cite_note-90]

Virginia's southern border [was defined in 1665](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Colonial_Boundary_of_1665) as [36°30' north latitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_36%C2%B030%E2%80%B2_north#In_the_United_States). Surveyors marking the border with North Carolina in the 18th century however started about 3.5 miles (5.6 km) to the north and drifted an additional 3.5 miles by [the border's westernmost point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Carolina%E2%80%93Tennessee%E2%80%93Virginia_Corners).[^cite_note-91] After [Tennessee](30395.md) joined the U.S. in 1796, new surveyors worked in 1802 and 1803 to reset their border with Virginia as a line from the summit of [White Top Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Top_Mountain) to the top of [Tri-State Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tri-State_Peak) in the [Cumberland Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Mountains). However, deviations in that border were identified when it was re-marked in 1856, and the Virginia General Assembly proposed a new surveying commission in 1871. Representatives from Tennessee preferred to keep the less-straight 1803 line, and in 1893, the [U.S. Supreme Court](31737.md) ruled for them [against Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_v._Tennessee).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Zandt197692–95-92][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith201571–72-93] One result is how the city of [Bristol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol,_Virginia) is divided in two between the states.[^cite_note-94]

### Geology and terrain

Main article: [Geology of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Virginia)

Rapids in a wide, rocky river under blue sky with clouds colored purple by the sunset.

[Great Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Falls_\(Potomac_River\)) is on the [fall line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard_Fall_Line) of the [Potomac River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potomac_River), and its rocks date to the late [Precambrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precambrian).

[^cite_note-95]

The [Chesapeake Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay) separates the contiguous portion of the Commonwealth from the two-county peninsula of Virginia's [Eastern Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Shore_of_Virginia). The bay was formed from the [drowned river valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ria) of the ancient [Susquehanna River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susquehanna_River).[^cite_note-bay-96] Many of [Virginia's rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_of_Virginia) flow into the Chesapeake Bay, including the [Potomac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potomac_River), [Rappahannock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rappahannock_River), [York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/York_River_\(Virginia\)), and [James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_River), which create three peninsulas in the bay, traditionally referred to as "necks" named [Northern Neck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Neck), [Middle Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Peninsula), and the [Virginia Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Peninsula) from north to south.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurnhamBurnham20181-97] [Sea level rise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level_rise) has eroded the land on Virginia's islands, which include [Tangier Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangier,_Virginia) in the bay and [Chincoteague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chincoteague,_Virginia), one of [23 barrier islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Barrier_Islands) on the Atlantic coast.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99]

The [Tidewater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidewater_\(geographic_term\)) is a [coastal plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_coastal_plain) between the Atlantic coast and the [fall line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard_fall_line). It includes the Eastern Shore and major [estuaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estuary) of Chesapeake Bay. The Piedmont is a series of [sedimentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sedimentary_rock) and [igneous rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igneous_rock)-based [foothills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foothills) east of the mountains.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPazzaglia2006135–138-100] The region, known for its heavy clay soil, includes the [Southwest Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_Mountains) around [Charlottesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville,_Virginia).[^cite_note-agriculture-101] The [Blue Ridge Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge_Mountains) are a [physiographic province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physiographic_regions_of_the_world) of the [Appalachian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_Mountains) with the highest points in the Commonwealth, the tallest being [Mount Rogers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Rogers_\(Virginia\)) at 5,729 feet (1,746 m).[^cite_note-mtrogers-2] The [Ridge-and-Valley region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridge-and-Valley_Appalachians) is west of the mountains, [carbonate rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbonate_rock) based, and includes the [Massanutten Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massanutten_Mountain) ridge and the [Great Appalachian Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Appalachian_Valley), which is called the [Shenandoah Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_Valley) in Virginia, named after the [river of the same name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_River) that flows through it.[^cite_note-regions-102] The [Cumberland Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Plateau) and [Cumberland Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Mountains) are in the southwest corner of Virginia, south of the [Allegheny Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegheny_Plateau). In this region, rivers flow northwest into the [Ohio River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River) basin.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPalmer199849–51-103]

[Virginia's seismic zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_seismic_zones) have not had a history of regular [earthquake](10106.md) activity. Earthquakes are rarely above 4.5 in [magnitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richter_magnitude_scale). The Commonwealth's largest earthquake in at least a century, at a magnitude of 5.8, [struck central Virginia on August 23, 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Virginia_earthquake).[^cite_note-104] 35 million years ago, a [bolide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolide) impacted what is now eastern Virginia. The resulting [Chesapeake Bay impact crater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay_impact_crater) may explain what earthquakes and [subsidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsidence) the region does experience.[^cite_note-105] A meteor impact is also theorized as the source of [Lake Drummond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Drummond), the largest of the two natural [lakes in the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_of_Virginia).[^cite_note-106]

The Commonwealth's carbonate rock is filled with more than 4,000 [limestone caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limestone_cave), ten of which are open for tourism, including the popular [Luray Caverns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luray_Caverns) and [Skyline Caverns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skyline_Caverns).[^cite_note-107] Virginia's iconic [Natural Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_Bridge_\(Virginia\)) is the remaining roof of a collapsed limestone cave.[^cite_note-108] [Coal mining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_mining_in_the_United_States) takes place in the three mountainous regions.[^cite_note-mining-109] More than 72 million tons of other non-fuel resources, such as [slate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slate), [kyanite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyanite), sand, or gravel, were mined in Virginia in 2020.[^cite_note-110] The largest known deposits of [uranium](31743.md) in the U.S. are under [Coles Hill, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coles_Hill_uranium_deposit). Despite [a challenge that reached the U.S. Supreme Court twice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Uranium,_Inc._v._Warren), the state has banned its mining since 1982 due to environmental and public health concerns.[^cite_note-111]

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Virginia)

See also: [Climate change in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Virginia)

| Virginia state-wide averages 1895–2023 |
| --- |
| Climate chart ([explanation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Climate_chart/How_to_read_a_climate_chart)) |
| J F M A M J J A S O N D        3.3     45 25       3.1     48 26       3.7     56 34       3.4     67 42       4     76 51       4.1     82 60       4.6     86 64       4.3     84 63       3.7     79 56       3.2     68 45       2.9     57 35       3.3     47 27   █ Average max. and min. temperatures in °F █ Precipitation totals in inchesSource: [U.S. Climate Divisional Dataset](#CITEREFNOAA) |
| Metric conversion  J F M A M J J A S O N D        84     7 −4       79     9 −3       94     14 1       86     19 6       102     24 11       104     28 15       117     30 18       109     29 17       94     26 14       81     20 7       74     14 1       84     8 −3   █ Average max. and min. temperatures in °C █ Precipitation totals in mm |

Virginia has a [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate) that transitions to [humid continental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate) west of the [Blue Ridge Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge_Mountains).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHamilton201612–13-112] Seasonal extremes vary from average lows of 25 °F (−4 °C) in January to average highs of 86 °F (30 °C) in July.[^cite_note-noaa-113] The Atlantic Ocean and [Gulf Stream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Stream) have a strong effect on eastern and southeastern coastal areas, making the climate there warmer but also more constant. Most of Virginia's recorded extremes in temperature and precipitation have occurred in the Blue Ridge Mountains and areas west.[^cite_note-Burnham-114] Virginia receives an average of 43.47 inches (110 cm) of precipitation annually,[^cite_note-noaa-113] with the [Shenandoah Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_Valley) being the state's driest region.[^cite_note-Burnham-114]

Virginia has around 35–45 days with thunderstorms annually, and storms are common in the late afternoon and evenings between April and September.[^cite_note-115] These months are also the most common for [tornadoes](37530.md),[^cite_note-116] six of which touched down in the Commonwealth in 2025.[^cite_note-117] [Hurricanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Virginia_hurricanes) and tropical storms can occur from August to October. The deadliest natural disaster in Virginia was [Hurricane Camille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Camille#Virginia), which killed over 150 people in 1969 mainly in inland [Nelson County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_County,_Virginia).[^cite_note-Burnham-114][^cite_note-118] Between December and March, [cold-air damming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold-air_damming) caused by the Appalachian Mountains can lead to significant snowfalls across the state, such as the [January 2016 blizzard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2016_United_States_blizzard), which created the state's highest recorded one-day snowfall of 36.6 inches (93 cm) near [Bluemont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluemont,_Virginia).[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] On average, cities in Virginia can receive between 5.8–12.3 inches (15–31 cm) of snow annually, but recent winters have seen below-average snowfalls, and much of Virginia had no measurable snow during the 2022–2023 winter season.[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122]

[Climate change in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Virginia) is leading to higher temperatures year-round as well as more heavy rain and flooding events.[^cite_note-123] [Urban heat islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_heat_island) can be found in many Virginia cities and suburbs, particularly in neighborhoods linked to historic [redlining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redlining).[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] The air in Virginia has statistically improved since 1998.[^cite_note-haze-126] The closure and conversion of [coal power plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_power_plant) in Virginia and the Ohio Valley region has helped cut the amount of [particulate matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Particulate_pollution) in Virginia's air in half.[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-eiaco-128] Current plans call for 30% of the Commonwealth's electricity to be renewable by 2030 and for all to be carbon-free by 2050.[^cite_note-129]

### Ecosystem

See also: [List of endangered species in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_endangered_species_in_Virginia)

A red-brown colored deer with antlers stands in a meadow with high grasses.

Up to 7,000 [white-tailed deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-tailed_deer), also known as Virginia deer, live in [Shenandoah National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_National_Park).

[^cite_note-gildard-130]

Forests cover 62% of Virginia as of 2021, of which 80% is considered [hardwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardwood) forest, meaning that trees are primarily [deciduous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deciduous) and [broad-leaved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad-leaved_tree). The other 20% is pine, with [loblolly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinus_taeda) and [shortleaf pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinus_echinata) dominating much of central and eastern Virginia.[^cite_note-stateoftheforest-131] In the western and mountainous parts of the Commonwealth, oak and hickory are most common, while lower altitudes are more likely to have small but dense stands of hemlocks and mosses in abundance.[^cite_note-Burnham-114] [Spongy moth infestations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gypsy_moths_in_the_United_States) in oak trees and the [blight in chestnut trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chestnut_blight) have decreased both of their numbers, leaving more room for hickory and the invasive [tree of heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ailanthus_altissima).[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-Burnham-114] In the lowland tidewater and [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont_\(plateau\)), yellow pines tend to dominate, with bald cypress wetland forests in the Great Dismal and Nottoway swamps.[^cite_note-stateoftheforest-131] Other common trees include red spruce, [Atlantic white cedar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamaecyparis_thyoides), [tulip-poplar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liriodendron_tulipifera), and the [flowering dogwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornus_florida), the [state tree and flower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_and_territory_trees).[^cite_note-133] Plants like [milkweed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asclepias), dandelions, daisies, ferns, and [Virginia creeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parthenocissus_quinquefolia), which is featured on the [state flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Virginia), are also common.[^cite_note-134] The [Thompson Wildlife Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G._Richard_Thompson_Wildlife_Management_Area) in [Fauquier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauquier_County,_Virginia) is known for having one of the largest populations of [trillium wildflowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trillium_grandiflorum) in North America.[^cite_note-Burnham-114]

[White-tailed deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-tailed_deer), one of 75 mammal species found in Virginia, rebounded from an estimated population of as few as 25,000 in the 1930s to over one million by the 2010s.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-mammals-136] Native [carnivorans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnivora) include [black bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_black_bear), who have a population of around five to six thousand in the state,[^cite_note-137] as well as [bobcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobcat), [coyotes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coyote), both [gray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray_fox) and [red foxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_fox), [raccoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raccoon), [weasels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weasel) and [skunks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skunk). Rodents include [groundhogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundhog), [nutria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nutria), [beavers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaver), both [gray squirrels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_gray_squirrel) and [fox squirrels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_squirrel), chipmunks, and [Allegheny woodrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegheny_woodrat), while the 17 bat species include brown bats and the [Virginia big-eared bat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_big-eared_bat), the [state mammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_mammals).[^cite_note-dwr_list-138][^cite_note-mammals-136] The [Virginia opossum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_opossum) is the only [marsupial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsupial) native to the United States and Canada,[^cite_note-139] and the native [Appalachian cottontail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_cottontail) was recognized in 1992 as a distinct species of rabbit, one of three found in the state.[^cite_note-140] Whales, dolphins, and porpoises have been recorded in Virginia's coastal waters, with [bottlenose dolphins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bottlenose_dolphins) being the most frequent [aquatic mammals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquatic_mammal).[^cite_note-mammals-136]

A gray and white bird of prey on the edge of a large nest with water in the distance.

[Osprey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osprey) nest at [False Cape State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Cape_State_Park) on a wooden platform designed to encourage their return to the area

[Virginia's bird fauna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Virginia) comprises 422 counted species, of which 359 are regularly occurring and 214 have bred in Virginia, while the rest are mostly [winter residents or transients](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird_migration).[^cite_note-Terwilliger-141] [Water birds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_birds) include sandpipers, wood ducks, and [Virginia rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_rail), while common inland examples include warblers, woodpeckers, and cardinals, the [state bird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_birds). Birds of prey include osprey, [broad-winged hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad-winged_hawk), and [barred owls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barred_owl).[^cite_note-142] There are no [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic_species) bird species.[^cite_note-Terwilliger-141] [Audubon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Audubon_Society) recognizes 21 [Important Bird Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Important_Bird_Area) in the state.[^cite_note-143] [Peregrine falcons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peregrine_falcon), whose numbers dramatically declined due to [DDT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DDT) poisoning in the middle of the 20th century, are the focus of conservation efforts in the state and a reintroduction program in [Shenandoah National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_National_Park).[^cite_note-144]

Virginia has 226 species of [freshwater fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freshwater_fish) from 25 families, a diversity attributable to the area's varied and humid climate, topography, interconnected river system, and lack of [Pleistocene glaciers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleistocene_glaciation). Common examples on the [Cumberland Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Plateau) and higher-elevation regions include [Eastern blacknose dace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_blacknose_dace), [sculpin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sculpin), [smallmouth bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smallmouth_bass), [redhorse sucker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redhorse_sucker), [Kanawha darter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanawha_darter), and [brook trout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brook_trout), the [state fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_fish). Downhill in the Piedmont, [stripeback darter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stripeback_darter) and [Roanoke bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke_bass) become common, as do [swampfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swampfish), [bluespotted sunfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluespotted_sunfish), and [pirate perch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pirate_perch) in the [Tidewater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidewater_\(region\)).[^cite_note-145] The [Chesapeake Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay) hosts clams, oysters, and 350 species of [saltwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saltwater_fish) and [estuarine fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_fish), including the bay's most abundant finfish, the [Bay anchovy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_anchovy), as well as the invasive [blue catfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_catfish).[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] An estimated 238 million [Chesapeake blue crabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Callinectes_sapidus) live in the bay as of 2025.[^cite_note-148] There are 34 native species of crayfish, like the [Big Sandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Sandy_crayfish).[^cite_note-149][^cite_note-Burnham-114] Amphibians found in Virginia include the [Cumberland Plateau salamander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Plateau_salamander) and [Eastern hellbender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_hellbender),[^cite_note-150] while the [northern watersnake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_watersnake) is the most common of the 32 snake species.[^cite_note-151]

### Protected lands

See also: [List of Virginia state parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Virginia_state_parks)

Five mountain ridges in shades of dark blue below an orange and yellow sunset.

[Oak](39696.md) trees produce a haze of [isoprene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isoprene), which helps give the [Blue Ridge Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge_Mountains) their signature color.

[^cite_note-152]

As of 2024, roughly 17.1% of land in the Commonwealth is protected by federal, state, and local governments and non-profits.[^cite_note-protectedland-153] Federal lands account for the majority, with 30 [National Park Service](161535.md) units, such as [Great Falls Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Falls_Park) and the [Appalachian Trail](87009.md), and one national park, [Shenandoah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_National_Park).[^cite_note-154] Almost 40 percent of Shenandoah's total 199,173 acres (806 km<sup>2</sup>) area has been designated as wilderness under the [National Wilderness Preservation System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Wilderness_Preservation_System).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarrollMiller2002158-155] The [U.S. Forest Service](42652.md) administers the [George Washington and Jefferson National Forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_and_Jefferson_National_Forests), which cover more than 1.6 million acres (6,500 km<sup>2</sup>) within Virginia's mountains, and continue into [West Virginia](32905.md) and [Kentucky](16846.md).[^cite_note-156] The [Great Dismal Swamp National Wildlife Refuge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Dismal_Swamp_National_Wildlife_Refuge) also extends into North Carolina, as does the [Back Bay National Wildlife Refuge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_Bay_National_Wildlife_Refuge), which marks the beginning of the [Outer Banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outer_Banks).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2008152–153,_356-157]

State agencies control about one-third of protected land in the state,[^cite_note-protectedland-153] and the [Virginia Department of Conservation and Recreation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Department_of_Conservation_and_Recreation) manages over 75,900 acres (307.2 km<sup>2</sup>) in 40 [Virginia state parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Virginia_state_parks) and 59,222 acres (239.7 km<sup>2</sup>) in 65 [Natural Area Preserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Natural_Area_Preserve_System), plus three undeveloped parks.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159] [Breaks Interstate Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breaks_Interstate_Park) crosses the Kentucky border and is one of only two inter-state parks in the United States.[^cite_note-160] Sustainable logging is allowed in 26 [state forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Virginia_state_forests) managed by the [Virginia Department of Forestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Department_of_Forestry) totaling 71,972 acres (291.3 km<sup>2</sup>),[^cite_note-forests-161] as is hunting in 44 [Wildlife Management Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Wildlife_Management_Areas) run by the [Virginia Department of Wildlife Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Department_of_Wildlife_Resources) covering over 205,000 acres (829.6 km<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-162] The [Chesapeake Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay) is not a national park, but is protected by both state and federal legislation and the inter-state [Chesapeake Bay Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay_Program).[^cite_note-163]

### Cities and towns

See also: [Political subdivisions of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_subdivisions_of_Virginia) and [Virginia statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_statistical_areas)

Map of Virginia counties colored by population density, ranging from pale yellow, to green, to dark blue.

The population density of Virginia counties and cities as of 2020

Virginia is divided into 95 [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Virginia) and 38 [independent cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_city_\(United_States\)), which the [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau) describes as [county-equivalents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County-equivalent).[^cite_note-164] This general method of treating cities and counties on par with each other is unique to Virginia and stretches back to the influence of [Williamsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williamsburg,_Virginia) and [Norfolk](57898.md) in the colonial period.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELibrary_of_Virginia1994183-165] Only three other [independent cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_city_\(United_States\)#Other_states) exist elsewhere in the US.[^cite_note-166] The differences between counties and cities in Virginia are small and have to do with how each assess new taxes, whether a referendum is necessary to issue bonds, and with the application of [Dillon's Rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dillon's_Rule), which limits the authority of cities and counties to countermand acts expressly allowed by the [General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_Assembly).[^cite_note-arlcity-167][^cite_note-168] Counties can also have [incorporated towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_towns_in_Virginia), and while there are no further [administrative subdivisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Virginia), the Census Bureau recognizes several hundred [unincorporated communities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_unincorporated_communities_in_Virginia).

An aerial view of several tall glassy office buildings.

[Arlington County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) in [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia) was once part of [Washington, D.C.](108956.md)

Over three million people, 35% of Virginians, live in the 20 jurisdictions collectively defined as [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia), part of the larger [Washington metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_metropolitan_area) and the [Northeast megalopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_megalopolis).[^cite_note-msa-169][^cite_note-170] [Fairfax County](91327.md), with more than 1.1 million residents, is Virginia's most populous jurisdiction,[^cite_note-wapo2018pop-171] and has a major urban business and shopping center in [Tysons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tysons,_Virginia), Virginia's largest office market.[^cite_note-172] Neighboring [Prince William County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_William_County,_Virginia), with over 450,000 residents, is Virginia's second-most populous county and home to [Marine Corps Base Quantico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Corps_Base_Quantico), the [FBI Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBI_Academy), and [Manassas National Battlefield Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manassas_National_Battlefield_Park). [Arlington County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) is the smallest self-governing county in the U.S. by land area,[^cite_note-173] and local politicians have proposed reorganizing it as an independent city due to its high density.[^cite_note-arlcity-167] [Loudoun County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudoun_County,_Virginia) is the fastest-growing county in the state.[^cite_note-wapo2018pop-171][^cite_note-174] In western Virginia, [Roanoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke,_Virginia) city and [Montgomery County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_County,_Virginia), part of the [Blacksburg–Christiansburg metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacksburg%E2%80%93Christiansburg_metropolitan_area), both have surpassed a population of 100,000 since 2018.[^cite_note-175]

On the western edge of the [Tidewater region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidewater_\(region\)) is Virginia's capital, [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia), which has a population of around 230,000 in its city proper and over 1.3 million in its metropolitan area. On the eastern edge is the [Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads) metropolitan area, where over 1.7 million reside across six counties and nine cities, including the Commonwealth's three most populous independent cities: [Virginia Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach,_Virginia), [Chesapeake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake,_Virginia), and [Norfolk](57898.md).[^cite_note-msa-169][^cite_note-agesex-176] Neighboring [Suffolk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suffolk,_Virginia), which includes a portion of the [Great Dismal Swamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Dismal_Swamp), is the largest city by area at 429.1 square miles (1,111 km<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-177] One reason for the concentration of independent cities in the Tidewater region is that several rural counties there re-incorporated as cities or consolidated with existing cities to try to hold on to their new suburban neighborhoods that started [booming in the 1950s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburbanization), since cities like Norfolk and [Portsmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portsmouth,_Virginia) were able to annex land from adjoining counties until a moratorium in 1987.[^cite_note-178] Others, like [Poquoson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poquoson,_Virginia), became cities to try to preserve [racial segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation) during the desegregation era of the 1970s.[^cite_note-179]
