The [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census) found the state resident population was 8,631,393, a 7.9% increase since the [2010 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census). Another 23,149 Virginians live overseas, giving the state a total population of 8,654,542. Virginia has the fourth-largest overseas population of U.S. states due to its federal employees and military personnel.[^cite_note-Bureau_2021-182] The [fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertility_rate) in Virginia as of 2020 was 55.8 per 1,000 females between the ages of 15 and 44,[^cite_note-183] and the [median age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_median_age) as of 2021 was the same as the national average of 38.8 years old.[^cite_note-agesex-176] The geographic [center of population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_of_population) is located in [Caroline County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_County,_Virginia), as of 2020.[^cite_note-\:0-184] As of 2020, [Virginia Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach,_Virginia) was most representative of the state's income, race, and level of educational attainment.[^cite_note-\:0-184]

Though still growing naturally as births outnumber deaths, Virginia has had a negative [net migration rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_migration_rate) since 2013, with 8,995 more people leaving the state than moving to it in 2021. This is largely credited to high home prices in Northern Virginia,[^cite_note-185] which are driving residents there to relocate south; [Raleigh](57845.md) is their top destination.[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187] Aside from Virginia, the top birth state for Virginians is [New York](8210131.md), with the [Northeast](431669.md) accounting for the largest number of domestic migrants into the state by region.[^cite_note-nytmigrationanddiaspora-188] About 12 percent of residents were born outside the United States as of 2020. [El Salvador](9356.md) is the most common foreign country of birth, with [India](14533.md), [Mexico](3966054.md), [South Korea](27019.md), the [Philippines](23440.md), and [Vietnam](202354.md) as other common birthplaces.[^cite_note-nova_immigrants-189]

### Race and ethnicity

[White non-Hispanics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_whites), the state's most populous racial group, have declined as a proportion of the population from 76% in 1990 to 58.6% in 2020.[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-race2020-191] Immigrants from Britain and Ireland settled throughout the Commonwealth during the colonial period,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillerSchrierBolingDoyle20036,_147-192] when roughly three-fourths of immigrants came as [indentured servants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indentured_servant).[^cite_note-193] The Appalachian mountains and [Shenandoah Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_Valley) have many settlements that were populated by [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_Germans) and [Scotch-Irish immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotch-Irish_Americans) in the 18th and 19th centuries, often following the [Great Wagon Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wagon_Road).[^cite_note-Dutch-194][^cite_note-195] Over ten percent of Virginians have German ancestry as of 2020.[^cite_note-ancestry-196]

Dozens of adults sit in auditorium rows, many waving small American flags

New citizens attend a [naturalization ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturalization_ceremony) in [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia), where 25% of residents are foreign-born, almost twice the overall state average.

[^cite_note-nova_immigrants-189]

The largest minority group in Virginia are Blacks and African Americans, about one-fifth of the population.[^cite_note-race2020-191] Virginia was a major destination of the [Atlantic slave trade](49005.md). The [Igbo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igbo_American) ethnic group of what is now southern [Nigeria](21383.md) were the largest African group among slaves in Virginia.[^cite_note-197] Blacks in Virginia also have more European ancestry than those in other southern states, and DNA analysis shows many have asymmetrical male and female ancestry from before the Civil War, evidence of European fathers and African or Native American mothers.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199] Though the Black population was reduced by the [Great Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Migration_\(African_American\)) to northern industrial cities in the first half of the 20th century, since 1965 there has been a reverse migration of Blacks [returning south](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Great_Migration).[^cite_note-200] The Commonwealth has the highest number of Black-White [interracial marriages in the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interracial_marriage_in_the_United_States),[^cite_note-blackwhite-201] and 8.2 percent of Virginians describe themselves as [multiracial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_people).[^cite_note-QuickFacts-3]

More recent immigration since the late 20th century has resulted in new communities of Hispanics and Asians. As of 2020, 10.5% of Virginia's total population describe themselves as [Hispanic or Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans), and 8.8% as [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_people).[^cite_note-QuickFacts-3] The state's Hispanic population rose by 92% from 2000 to 2010, with two-thirds of Hispanics in the state living in [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia).[^cite_note-demographics-202] Northern Virginia also has a significant population of [Vietnamese Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_American), whose major wave of immigration followed the [Vietnam War](32611.md).[^cite_note-203] [Korean Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_American) have migrated there more recently,[^cite_note-204] while about 45,000 [Filipino Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_American) have settled in the Hampton Roads area.[^cite_note-205]

An older man in a dark blue blazer smiles as he is presented with a dead deer hanging upside down held by two men in contemporary Native American attire.

Governor [Glenn Youngkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Youngkin) receiving a ceremonial tribute from representatives of the [Mattaponi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattaponi) and [Pamunkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamunkey) tribes, a Thanksgiving tradition since 1677

[^cite_note-taxtribute-206]

[Tribal membership in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_tribes_in_Virginia) is complicated by the legacy of the state's "[pencil genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_Integrity_Act_of_1924)" of intentionally categorizing Native Americans and Blacks together, and many tribal members do have African or European ancestry, or both.[^cite_note-207] In 2020, the U.S. Census Bureau found that only 0.5% of Virginians were exclusively [American Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) or [Alaska Native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Native), though 2.1% were in some combination with other ethnicities.[^cite_note-race2020-191] The state government has [extended recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-recognized_tribes_in_the_United_States) to 11 tribes. Seven tribes also have federal recognition.[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209] The [Pamunkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamunkey) and [Mattaponi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattaponi) have reservations on tributaries of the [York River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/York_River_\(Virginia\)) in the [Tidewater region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidewater_region_of_Virginia).[^cite_note-210]

### Languages

A recording of a resident of [Tangier Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangier,_Virginia) who was born in the late 1800s, showcasing the island's unique accent

According to U.S. Census data as of 2022 on Virginia residents aged five and older, 83% (6,805,548) speak [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) at home as a [first language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_language). [Spanish](26825.md) is the next most commonly spoken language, with 7.5% (611,831) of Virginia households, though age is a factor; 8.7% (120,560) of Virginians under age 18 speak Spanish. [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language) was the third most commonly spoken language with around 0.8% of residents, followed by [Chinese languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language) and [Vietnamese](32511.md) each with over 0.7%, and then [Korean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_language) and [Tagalog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagalog_language), just under 0.7% and 0.6% respectively.[^cite_note-211]

English was passed as the Commonwealth's official language by statutes in 1981 and again in 1996, though the status is not mandated by the constitution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph200663-212] While a more homogenized [American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_English) is found in urban areas, and the use of Southern accents in general has been on the decline in speakers born since the 1960s,[^cite_note-213] various accents are still present.[^cite_note-pleasant-214] The [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont_region_of_Virginia) region is known for its non-rhotic dialect's strong influence on [Southern American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_American_English), and a [BBC America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_America) study in 2014 ranked it as one of the most identifiable accents in American English.[^cite_note-215] The [Tidewater accent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidewater_accent) evolved from the language that upper-class English typically spoke in the early Colonial period, while the [Appalachian accent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_English) has much more influence from the English spoken by Scottish and Irish immigrants from that time.[^cite_note-pleasant-214][^cite_note-216] [Appalachian stereotypes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_stereotypes) have, however, led to some from the region [code-switching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Code-switching) to a less distinct English accent.[^cite_note-217] The English spoken on [Tangier Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangier,_Virginia) in the [Chesapeake Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake_Bay), preserved by the island's isolation, contains many phrases and euphemisms not found anywhere else and retains elements of [Early Modern English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Modern_English).[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-Miller-219]

### Religion

See also: [Religion in early Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_early_Virginia)

**Religious tradition** ([2025](#CITEREFprri2025))

1. [Unaffiliated/Don't Know](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_the_United_States): 28%

2. [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_the_United_States): 43%

3. [Catholicism](410868.md): 18%

4. [Judaism](1131183.md): 3%

5. [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah's_Witnesses): 2%

6. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_the_United_States): 2%

7. [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_the_United_States): 1%

8. [Mormonism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormonism): 1%

9. [Unitarian Universalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitarian_Universalism): 1%

10. Other Non-Christian: 1%

Virginia enshrined [religious freedom](46006.md) in [a 1786 statute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Statute_for_Religious_Freedom). Though the state is historically part of America's [Bible Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible_Belt), the 2025 [Public Religion Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Religion_Research_Institute) (PRRI) survey estimated that 49% of Virginians either seldom or never attend religious services and that the percent of Virginians unaffiliated with any particular religious body had increased from 21% in 2013 to 27% in 2025.[^cite_note-prri2025-220] The 2020 U.S. Religion Census conducted by the [Association of Religion Data Archives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Religion_Data_Archives) (ARDA) similarly found that 55% of Virginians attend none of the state's 10,477 congregations.[^cite_note-whopractices-221] Overall [belief in God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belief_in_God) has also declined in [the South region](179553.md), of which Virginia is a part, from 93% of respondents in [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc.) surveys from 2013 to 2017, to 86% in 2022.[^cite_note-222]

Of the 45% of Virginians who were associated with religious bodies in the 2020 ARDA census, [Evangelical Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) made up the largest overall grouping, with 20.3% of the state's population, while 8.1% and 2% were [mainline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant) and [Black Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_church) respectively. [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists), 84% of which are counted as Evangelical, included 9.4% of Virginians in that census.[^cite_note-arda2020-223] Their major division is between the [Baptist General Association of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist_General_Association_of_Virginia), which formed in 1823, and the [Southern Baptist Conservatives of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Baptist_Conservatives_of_Virginia), which split off in 1996. Other Protestant branches with over one percent of Virginians included [Pentecostalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostalism) (1.8%), [Presbyterianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterianism) (1.3%), [Anglicanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglicanism) (1.2%), and [Adventism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adventism) (1%).[^cite_note-arda2020-223] The 2025 PRRI survey estimated that 43% of Virginians were Protestants, mainly split into 15% White Evangelical, 14% White Mainline, and 11% Black Protestant, though these numbers include individuals who report not attending services.[^cite_note-prri2025-220]

An outdoor auditorium with seated guests lined with neoclassical columns and a closed archway on one side and banners hanging inside the arch.

Since 1927, [Arlington National Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_National_Cemetery) in [Arlington County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) has hosted an annual nondenominational [sunrise service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunrise_service) every Easter.

[^cite_note-224]

[Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_the_United_States) accounted for 10.3% in the 2020 ARDA census,[^cite_note-arda2020-223] and 18% in the 2023 PRRI survey, which divided them into 10% White Catholic, 6% Hispanic Catholic, and 2% other.[^cite_note-prri2025-220] Catholic churches are organized in either the [Diocese of Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Arlington) or [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Richmond), while [Episcopal churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Church_\(United_States\)) are similarly in their [Diocese of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Diocese_of_Virginia), [Southern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Diocese_of_Southern_Virginia), and [Southwestern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Diocese_of_Southwestern_Virginia). Adherents of [the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints) constitute just over one percent of the population, with 212 [congregations in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints_in_Virginia) as of 2026.[^cite_note-225] While the state's Jewish population is small, organized Jewish sites date to 1789 with [Congregation Beth Ahabah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_Beth_Ahabah).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOlitzky1996359-226]

[Fairfax County](91327.md) is the state's most religiously diverse jurisdiction.[^cite_note-whopractices-221] [Fairfax Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairfax_Station,_Virginia) is the site of the [Ekoji Buddhist Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekoji_Buddhist_Temple), of the [Jōdo Shinshū](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C5%8Ddo_Shinsh%C5%AB) school, and the Hindu [Durga Temple of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Temple_of_Virginia). The [All Dulles Area Muslim Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Dulles_Area_Muslim_Society), on the county's border in [Sterling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sterling,_Virginia), considers its 11 branches the country's second-largest [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) mosque community.[^cite_note-227] [McLean Bible Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLean_Bible_Church), with around 16,500 weekly visitors, is among the top 25 largest [megachurches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megachurch) in the U.S. and 8.4% of Virginians attend [nondenomination Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nondenominational_Christianity) churches like it, according to the 2020 ARDA census.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-arda2020-223] [Lynchburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynchburg_metropolitan_area) and [Roanoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke_metropolitan_area) ranked in that census as the two metropolitan areas with the highest rates of religious adherence, while the state-college-dominated [Blacksburg–Christiansburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacksburg%E2%80%93Christiansburg_metropolitan_area) and [Charlottesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville,_Virginia_metropolitan_area) were the lowest.[^cite_note-arda2020-223] Two major Christian universities, [Liberty University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_University) and the [University of Lynchburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Lynchburg), are based in Lynchburg, while [Regent University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regent_University) is in [Virginia Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach,_Virginia).

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Virginia)

See also: [Virginia locations by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_locations_by_per_capita_income)

Map of Virginia counties colored by median household income, ranging from gray, to blue, to darker green.

Counties and cities by median household income between 2015 and 2019

Virginia's economy has diverse sources of income, including local and federal government, military, farming and high-tech. In 2025, Virginia's per capita personal income was \$80,291[^cite_note-U.S._Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis-229] and the state's [gross domestic product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_GDP) (GDP) was \$798.4 billion.[^cite_note-U.S._Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis-229] The [COVID-19 recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_recession) caused jobless claims due to soar over 10% in early April 2020,[^cite_note-230] returning to pre-pandemic levels in 2023.[^cite_note-231] In June 2026, the unemployment rate was 3.7%, which was the 22nd-lowest nationwide.[^cite_note-232]

Virginia has a [median household income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Median_household_income) of \$89,931, as of 2023, 11th-highest nationwide,[^cite_note-mhi-4] and a poverty rate of 10.2%, [10th-lowest nationwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_poverty_rate).[^cite_note-QuickFacts-3] [Montgomery County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_County,_Virginia) outside [Blacksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacksburg,_Virginia) has the highest poverty rate in the state, with 28.5% falling below the [U.S. Census poverty thresholds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_thresholds_\(United_States_Census_Bureau\)). [Loudoun County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudoun_County,_Virginia) meanwhile has the highest median household income in the nation, and the wider [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia) region is among the highest-income regions nationwide.[^cite_note-poverty-233] As of 2022, 18 of the 100 [highest-income counties in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highest-income_counties_in_the_United_States), including the two highest, are located in Northern Virginia.[^cite_note-234] Though [median home prices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_median_home_price) in Virginia are generally above the national average, particularly in Northern Virginia, where they were 44.8% higher in May 2024, at \$760,000,[^cite_note-235] 69.1% of [Virginians own their home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_Virginia) as of 2023.[^cite_note-236] The [Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads) region has the state's highest per capita number of homeless individuals, with 11 per 10,000, as of 2020.[^cite_note-237] Though the [Gini index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_Gini_coefficient) shows Virginia has less [income inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_inequality) than the national average,[^cite_note-238] the state's middle class is also smaller than the majority of states.[^cite_note-247wallst-239]

[CNBC](223441.md) ranked Virginia third in their 2026 [Top State for Business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America's_Top_States_For_Business), with its deductions being mainly a result of cuts to the federal workforce under President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [D.O.G.E.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Government_Efficiency),[^cite_note-240] while *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* magazine ranked it as the 16th best to start a business in.[^cite_note-241] In 2025, 99.6% of businesses in Virginia were [small businesses](276495.md) and employed 45.9% of the state's workforce.[^cite_note-242] [Oxfam America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfam_America) however ranked Virginia in 2025 as only the 23rd-best state to work in, with pluses for worker protections from sexual harassment and [pregnancy discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pregnancy_discrimination), but negatives for laws on organized labor and the low tipped employee [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage) of \$2.13.[^cite_note-oxfam-243] Virginia has been an [employment-at-will](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/At-will_employment) state since 1906 and a "[right to work](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-to-work_law)" state since 1947,[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245] and though state minimum wage increased to \$12 in 2023, farm and tipped workers are specifically excluded.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-oxfam-243]

### Government agencies

Aerial view of the huge five-sided building and its multiple rings. Parking lots and highways stretch away from it.

The [U.S. Department of Defense](7279897.md) is headquartered in [Arlington County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) at [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon).

Government agencies directly employ around 714,100 Virginians as of 2022, almost 17% of all employees in the state.[^cite_note-247] Approximately 12% of all [U.S. federal procurement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_procurement_in_the_United_States) money is spent in Virginia, the second-highest amount after [California](5407.md).[^cite_note-fedspend-248][^cite_note-weighdefense-249] As of 2020, 125,648 active-duty personnel, 25,404 reservists, and 99,832 civilians work directly for the [U.S. Department of Defense](7279897.md) at [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) or one of 27 military bases in the state covering 270,009 acres (1,092.69 km<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-mil-250] Another 139,000 Virginians work for [defense contracting](13433595.md) firms,[^cite_note-251] which received \$44.8 billion worth of contracts in the 2020 fiscal year.[^cite_note-mil-250] Virginia has the second highest concentration of veterans of any state with 9.7% of the population. The [Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads) area is home to the world's largest navy base and only [NATO](21133.md) station on U.S. soil, [Naval Station Norfolk](896196.md).[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-mil-250]

Other large [federal agencies in Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_federal_agencies_in_Northern_Virginia) include the [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) in [Langley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langley,_Virginia), the [National Science Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Science_Foundation) and [U.S. Patent and Trademark Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Patent_and_Trademark_Office) in [Alexandria](18940583.md), the [U.S. Geological Survey](23814944.md) in [Reston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reston,_Virginia), and the [U.S. Fish & Wildlife Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Fish_&_Wildlife_Service) in [Bailey's Crossroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bailey's_Crossroads,_Virginia). Virginia's state government employs over 106,000 public employees, who combined have a median income of \$52,401 as of 2018,[^cite_note-253] with the [Departments of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Department_of_Transportation) and of [Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Department_of_Education) the two largest state departments by expenditure.[^cite_note-254] K–12 teachers in Virginia make an annual average of \$59,970, which is 13th-lowest in the U.S. when adjusted for the state's [cost of living](522934.md) as of the 2021–22 school year.[^cite_note-255]

### Business

High-rise hotels line the ocean front covered with colorful beach-goers.

Ocean tourism is an important sector of [Virginia Beach's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach,_Virginia) economy.

Based on data as of 2020, Virginia is home to 204,131 separate employers plus 644,341 [sole proprietorships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sole_proprietorship). Of the 144,431 registered non-farm businesses in 2017, 59.4% are majority male-owned, 22% are majority female-owned, 19.6% are majority minority-owned, and 8.9% are veteran-owned.[^cite_note-QuickFacts-3] Twenty-four [Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies are headquartered in Virginia as of 2024, with the largest companies by revenue being [Freddie Mac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freddie_Mac), [Boeing](18933266.md), [RTX Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation), [Performance Food Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performance_Food_Group), and [Capital One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_One).[^cite_note-256] The two largest by number of employees are [Dollar Tree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dollar_Tree) in [Chesapeake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapeake,_Virginia) and [Hilton Worldwide Holdings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilton_Worldwide_Holdings) in [McLean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLean,_Virginia).[^cite_note-257]

Virginia has the third highest concentration of technology workers and the fifth highest overall number among U.S. states as of 2020, with the 451,268 tech jobs accounting for 11.1% of all jobs in the state and earning a median salary of \$98,292.[^cite_note-cyberstates-258] Many of these jobs are in [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia), which hosts a large number of software, communications, and cybersecurity companies, particularly in the [Dulles Technology Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dulles_Technology_Corridor) and [Tysons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tysons,_Virginia) areas. [Amazon](90451.md) additionally selected [Crystal City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crystal_City,_Virginia) for [its HQ2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_HQ2) in 2018, while [Google](1092923.md) expanded their [Reston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reston,_Virginia) offices in 2019.

Northern Virginia became the world's largest [data center](579730.md) market in 2016, with over 47.7 million square feet (4.43 km<sup>2</sup>) as of 2023,[^cite_note-259] much of it in [Loudoun County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudoun_County,_Virginia), which has branded itself "Data Center Alley".[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] Data centers in Virginia handled around one-third of all internet traffic and directly employed 13,500 Virginians in 2023 and supported 45,000 total jobs.[^cite_note-262] Virginia had the second fastest average internet speed among U.S. states that year and ninth highest percent of households with broadband access, at 93.6%.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264] [Computer chips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_circuit) became the state's highest-grossing export in 2006,[^cite_note-chips-265] and had an estimated export value of \$740 million in 2022.[^cite_note-chips_census-266] Though in the top quartile for diversity based on the [Simpson index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simpson_index), only 26% of tech employees in Virginia are women, and only 13% are Black or African American.[^cite_note-cyberstates-258]

Tourists spent a record \$33.3 billion in Virginia in 2023, an increase of 10% from the previous year, supporting an estimated 224,000 jobs, an increase of 13,000.[^cite_note-267] The state ranked as the eighth most visited based on data from 2022.[^cite_note-268] That year saw 745,000 international visitors, with 41% coming from [Canada](5042916.md).[^cite_note-269]

### Agriculture

Two adult men in green and red baseball caps work with their hands while crouching down in a field of wide green leaves.

[Rockingham County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockingham_County,_Virginia) in the [Shenandoah Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_Valley) accounts for 20 percent of Virginia's agricultural sales as of 2017, with the valley as a whole being the state's most productive region.

[^cite_note-270]

As of 2021, agriculture occupies 30% of the land in Virginia with 7.7 million acres (12,031 sq mi; 31,161 km<sup>2</sup>) of farmland. Nearly 54,000 Virginians work on the state's 41,500 farms, which average 186 acres (0.29 sq mi; 0.75 km<sup>2</sup>). Though agriculture has declined significantly since 1960, when there were twice as many farms, it remains the largest industry in Virginia, providing for over 490,000 jobs.[^cite_note-agfacts-271] Soybeans were the most profitable single crop in Virginia in 2022,[^cite_note-Virginia's_Top_20_Farm_Commodities-272] although the ongoing [trade war with China](57055272.md) has led many Virginia farmers to plant cotton instead.[^cite_note-273] Other leading agricultural products include corn, cut flowers, and tobacco, where the state ranks third nationally in [production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultivation_of_tobacco).[^cite_note-agfacts-271][^cite_note-Virginia's_Top_20_Farm_Commodities-272]

Virginia is the country's third-largest producer of seafood as of 2021, with [sea scallops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Placopecten_magellanicus), oysters, [Chesapeake blue crabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Callinectes_sapidus), [menhaden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menhaden), and hardshell clams as the largest seafood harvests by value, and [France](5843419.md), [Canada](5042916.md), [New Zealand](4913064.md), and [Hong Kong](13404.md) as the top export destinations.[^cite_note-274] Commercial fishing supports 18,220 jobs as of 2020, while recreation fishing supports another 5,893.[^cite_note-275] The population of [eastern oysters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_oyster) collapsed in the 1980s due to pollution and overharvesting, but has slowly rebounded, and the 2022–2023 season saw the largest harvest in 35 years with around 700,000 US bushels (25,000 kL).[^cite_note-276] A warm winter and a dry summer made the 2023 wine harvest one of the best for vineyards in the [Northern Neck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Neck) and along the [Blue Ridge Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge_Mountains), which also attract 2.6 million tourists annually.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-wine-278] Virginia has the seventh-highest number of wineries in the nation, with 388 producing 1.1 million cases a year as of 2024.[^cite_note-279] [Virginia's 344 craft breweries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_breweries_in_Virginia) also produced 342,075 barrels (40,141 kl) of craft beer in 2025, the 18th-most nationally.[^cite_note-280]

### Taxes

A map of Virginia colored green to blue based on how much property tax was paid, from \$200 to \$4,000+.

Counties and cities by median [property tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax) paid in 2019

[State income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_income_tax) is collected from those with incomes above a [filing threshold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_return_\(United_States\)). There are five income brackets, with rates ranging from 2.0% to 5.75% of taxable income.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] The [state sales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_taxes_in_the_United_States) and [use tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_tax) rate is 4.3%, though there is an additional 1% local tax, for a total of a 5.3% combined [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax) on most purchases. Three regions then have a higher sales tax: 6% in Northern Virginia and Hampton Roads, and 7% in the [Historic Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Triangle).[^cite_note-Retail-283] Unlike the majority of states, Virginia does have a 1% sales tax on groceries.[^cite_note-284] This was lowered from 2.5% in January 2023, when the items covered by this lower rate were also extended to include essential personal hygiene goods.[^cite_note-Retail-283][^cite_note-285]

Virginia's [property tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax_in_the_United_States) is set and collected at the local government level and varies throughout the Commonwealth. Real estate is also taxed at the local level.[^cite_note-ceps-286] As of 2021, the overall median real estate tax rate per \$100 of assessed taxable value was \$0.96, though for 72 of the 95 counties this number was under \$0.80 per \$100. Northern Virginia has the highest property taxes in the state, with [Manassas Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manassas_Park,_Virginia) paying the highest effective tax rate at \$1.31 per \$100, while [Powhatan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powhatan_County,_Virginia) and [Lunenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunenburg_County,_Virginia) counties were tied for the lowest, at \$0.30.[^cite_note-287] Of local government tax revenue, about 61% is generated from real property taxes while 24% is from tangible personal property, sales and use, and business license tax. The remaining 15% come from [taxes on hotels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotel_tax), restaurant meals, public service corporation property, and consumer utilities.[^cite_note-ceps-286]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Virginia)

Five women dressed in long colonial style clothing sit on the stairs of tan and beige buildings talking. In front of them is a wooden wheelbarrow full of wicker baskets.

Colonial Virginian culture, language, and style are reenacted in [Williamsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_Williamsburg).

Modern Virginian culture has many sources and is part of the [culture of the Southern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_Southern_United_States).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFischerKelly2000102–103-288] The [Smithsonian Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian_Institution) divides Virginia into nine cultural regions.[^cite_note-289]

Besides the general [cuisine of the Southern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_the_Southern_United_States), Virginians maintain their own particular traditions. [Virginia wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_wine) is made in many parts of the Commonwealth.[^cite_note-wine-278] [Smithfield ham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithfield_ham), sometimes called "Virginia ham", is a type of [country ham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_ham) which is [protected by state law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_indication) and can be produced only in the town of [Smithfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithfield,_Virginia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamson200841-290] [Virginia furniture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_furniture) and architecture are typical of [American colonial architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_colonial_architecture). Thomas Jefferson and many of the Commonwealth's early leaders favored the [Neoclassical architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassical_architecture) style, leading to its use for important state buildings. The [Pennsylvania Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Dutch) and their style can also be found in parts of the Commonwealth.[^cite_note-Dutch-194]

Literature in Virginia often deals with the Commonwealth's past. The works of [Pulitzer Prize](24230.md) winner [Ellen Glasgow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellen_Glasgow) often dealt with social inequalities and the role of women in her culture.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrayRobinson200481,_103-291] [James Branch Cabell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Branch_Cabell) wrote extensively about the changing position of gentry in the [Reconstruction era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_era), and challenged its moral code with *[Jurgen, A Comedy of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurgen,_A_Comedy_of_Justice)*.[^cite_note-292] [William Styron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Styron) approached history in works such as *[The Confessions of Nat Turner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Confessions_of_Nat_Turner_\(1967\))* and *[Sophie's Choice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophie's_Choice_\(novel\))*.[^cite_note-293] [Tom Wolfe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Wolfe) has occasionally dealt with his southern heritage in bestsellers like *[I Am Charlotte Simmons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Am_Charlotte_Simmons)*.[^cite_note-294] [Matt Bondurant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Bondurant) received critical acclaim for his [historic novel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_fiction) *[The Wettest County in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wettest_County_in_the_World)* about moonshiners in [Franklin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_County,_Virginia) during [prohibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prohibition_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-295] Virginia also names a [state Poet Laureate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poet_Laureate_of_Virginia).[^cite_note-296]

### Fine and performing arts

See also: [Music of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Virginia)

Five male musicians perform on stage in front of a standing audience, behind them a dozen lights project blue lines upward.

[The Steel Wheels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Steel_Wheels), an Americana roots folk rock band, plays at [Jefferson Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_Theater_\(Virginia\)) in [Charlottesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville,_Virginia) in February 2019.

Virginia ranks near the middle of U.S. states in terms of public spending on the arts as of 2021, at just over half of the national average.[^cite_note-297] The state government does fund some institutions, including the [Virginia Museum of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Museum_of_Fine_Arts) and the [Science Museum of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_Museum_of_Virginia). Other museums include the [Steven F. Udvar-Hazy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_F._Udvar-Hazy_Center) of the [National Air and Space Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Air_and_Space_Museum) and the [Chrysler Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrysler_Museum_of_Art).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith200822–25-298] Besides these sites, many open-air museums are located in the Commonwealth, such as [Colonial Williamsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_Williamsburg), the [Frontier Culture Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontier_Culture_Museum_of_Virginia), and various historic battlefields.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHowardBurnhamBurnham200688,_206,_292-299] The [Virginia Foundation for the Humanities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Foundation_for_the_Humanities) works to improve the Commonwealth's civic, cultural, and intellectual life.[^cite_note-300]

The [Harrison Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison_Opera_House), in [Norfolk](57898.md), is home of the [Virginia Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Opera). The [Virginia Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Symphony_Orchestra) operates in and around [Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHowardBurnhamBurnham2006165–166-301] Resident and touring theater troupes operate from the [American Shakespeare Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Shakespeare_Center) in [Staunton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staunton,_Virginia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodwin2012154-302] The [Barter Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barter_Theatre) in [Abingdon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abingdon,_Virginia), designated the State Theatre of Virginia, won the first [Regional Theatre Tony Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Theatre_Tony_Award) in 1948, while the [Signature Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signature_Theatre_\(Arlington,_Virginia\)) in [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) won it in 2009. There is also a Children's Theater of Virginia, [Theatre IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_IV), which is the second-largest touring troupe in the nation.[^cite_note-303] Notable music performance venues include [The Birchmere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Birchmere), the [Landmark Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landmark_Theater_\(Richmond,_Virginia\)), and [Jiffy Lube Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiffy_Lube_Live).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHowardBurnhamBurnham200629,_121,_363,_432-304] [Wolf Trap National Park for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolf_Trap_National_Park_for_the_Performing_Arts) is located in [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna,_Virginia) and is the only national park intended for use as a performing arts center.[^cite_note-wolftrap-305]

Virginia is known for its tradition in the music genres of [old-time string](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old-time_music) and [bluegrass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluegrass_music), with groups such as the [Carter Family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carter_Family) and [Stanley Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Brothers) achieving national prominence during the 1940s.[^cite_note-306] The state's African tradition is found through [gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_\(music\)), [blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues), and [shout bands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shout_band), with both [Ella Fitzgerald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ella_Fitzgerald) and [Pearl Bailey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Bailey) coming from [Newport News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport_News,_Virginia).[^cite_note-307] Contemporary Virginia is also known for [folk rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_rock) artists like [Dave Matthews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Matthews) and [Jason Mraz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Mraz), [R\&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R\&B) artists [Chris Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Brown), [D'Angelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D'Angelo), and [Kali Uchis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kali_Uchis), [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop) stars like [Pharrell Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharrell_Williams), [Timbaland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timbaland), [Missy Elliott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missy_Elliott) and [Pusha T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pusha_T), as well as [thrash metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrash_metal) groups like [Gwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwar), [Lamb of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_of_God_\(band\)) and [metalcore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metalcore) acts like [Bad Omens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Omens).[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] Several members of [country music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music) band [Old Dominion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Dominion_\(band\)) grew up in the [Roanoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke,_Virginia) area, and took their band name from Virginia's state nickname.[^cite_note-310]

### Festivals

Dozens of brown and white ponies surge out of the shallow water onto a grassy shore crowded with onlookers.

The annual [Pony Penning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pony_Penning) features more than 200 [wild ponies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chincoteague_Pony) swimming across the [Assateague Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assateague_Channel) into [Chincoteague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chincoteague,_Virginia).

[^cite_note-ponies-311]

Many counties and localities host [county fairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_fair) and festivals. The [Virginia State Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_State_Fair) is held at the [Meadow Event Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meadow_Event_Park) every September. Also in September is the [Neptune Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neptune_Festival) in [Virginia Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach,_Virginia), which celebrates the city, the waterfront, and regional artists. [Norfolk's Harborfest](57898.md#Parks_and_recreation), in June, features boat racing and [air shows](2790.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodwin201225,_287-312] [Fairfax County](91327.md) also sponsors Celebrate Fairfax! with popular and traditional music performances.[^cite_note-313] The Virginia Lake Festival is held in July in [Clarksville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarksville,_VA).[^cite_note-314] The [Eastern Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Shore_of_Virginia) island of [Chincoteague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chincoteague,_Virginia) hosts the annual [Pony Penning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pony_Penning) of feral [Chincoteague ponies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chincoteague_Pony), expanded into a week-long carnival.[^cite_note-ponies-311] Every year on [Thanksgiving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanksgiving_\(United_States\)) in [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia), the [Mattaponi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattaponi) and [Pamunkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamunkey) tribes present Virginia's governor with a tribute of deer in a celebration honoring colonial treaties.[^cite_note-taxtribute-206]

The [Shenandoah Apple Blossom Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenandoah_Apple_Blossom_Festival) is a two-week festival held annually in [Winchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winchester,_Virginia) which includes parades and [bluegrass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluegrass_music) concerts. The [Old Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old-time_music) Fiddlers' Convention in [Galax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galax,_Virginia), begun in 1935, is one of the oldest and largest such events worldwide. Wolf Trap hosts the [Wolf Trap Opera Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolf_Trap_Opera_Company), which produces an opera festival every summer.[^cite_note-wolftrap-305] The Blue Ridge Rock Festival has operated since 2017, and has brought as many as 33,000 concert-goers to the Blue Ridge Amphitheater in [Pittsylvania County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsylvania_County,_Virginia).[^cite_note-315] Two important film festivals, the [Virginia Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Film_Festival) and the [VCU French Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VCU_French_Film_Festival), are held annually in [Charlottesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville,_Virginia) and Richmond, respectively.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodwin201225–26-316] The [Something in the Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Something_in_the_Water_\(festival\)) has also been a major festival in Virginia Beach, hosted by [Pharrell Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharrell_Williams), and has been running since 2019.

## Law and government

Main articles: [Law of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Virginia) and [Government of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Virginia)

An all white Neoclassical building with pediment and six columns rises on a grassy hill with a large American elm tree in the left foreground. Two boxier, but similarly styled wings are attached at the building's rear.

The [Virginia State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_State_Capitol) in [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia), designed by [Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson) and [Charles-Louis Clérisseau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles-Louis_Cl%C3%A9risseau), is home to the [Virginia General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_Assembly).

In 1619, the first [Virginia General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_Assembly) met, making Virginia's legislature the oldest of its kind in North America.[^cite_note-hemisphere-317] The government today functions under the seventh [Constitution of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Virginia), which was [approved by voters in 1970](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_Virginia_ballot_measures) and went into effect in July 1971.[^cite_note-constitution_1970-79] It is similar to the [federal structure](195149.md) in that it provides for [three branches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers): a strong legislature, an executive, and a unified judicial system.[^cite_note-constitution-318]

Virginia's legislature is [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameral), with a 100-member [House of Delegates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_House_of_Delegates) and 40-member [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_Virginia), who together write the laws for the Commonwealth. Delegates serve two-year terms, while senators serve four-year terms, with [the most recent elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Virginia_elections) for each taking place in November 2025 and 2023 respectively. The executive department includes the [governor](253093.md), [lieutenant governor](1461923.md), and [attorney general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_Virginia), who are elected every four years in separate elections, with the [most recent taking place in November 2025](69194251.md). [Incumbent governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors_of_Virginia) cannot run for re-election; governors can and have served non-consecutive terms.[^cite_note-319] The lieutenant governor is the official head of the Senate and is responsible for breaking ties. The House elects a [Speaker of the House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Speakers_of_the_Virginia_House_of_Delegates) and the Senate elects a [President pro tempore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_pro_tempore_of_the_Senate_of_Virginia), who presides when the lieutenant governor is not present, and both houses elect a clerk and majority and minority leaders.[^cite_note-vga_guide-320] The governor also nominates their 16 [cabinet members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Governor's_Cabinet) and others who head various state departments.[^cite_note-321]

The legislature starts regular sessions on the second Wednesday of every year. They meet for up to 48 days in odd years, which are election years, or 60 days in even years, to allow more time for biennial state budgets, which governors propose.[^cite_note-vga_guide-320][^cite_note-322] After regular sessions end, special sessions can be called either by the governor or with agreement of two-thirds of both houses, and 21 special sessions have been called since 2000, typically for legislation on preselected issues.[^cite_note-323] Though not a full-time legislature, the Assembly is classified as a hybrid because special sessions are not limited by the state constitution and often last several months.[^cite_note-324] A one-day "veto session" is also automatically triggered when a governor chooses to veto or return legislation to the Assembly with amendments. Vetoes can then be overturned with approval of two-thirds of both the House and Senate.[^cite_note-325] A bill that passes with two-thirds approval can also become law without action from the governor,[^cite_note-326] and Virginia has no "[pocket veto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pocket_veto)", so bills become law if the governor chooses to neither approve nor veto them.[^cite_note-327]

### Legal system

A seven-story sandstone building faced with ionic columns on a city street corner.

Unlike the [federal judiciary system](1636627.md), justices of the [Virginia Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Virginia) have term limits, a mandatory retirement age, and select their own Chief Justice.

The judges and justices who make up [Virginia's judicial system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary_of_Virginia), also the oldest in America, are elected by a majority vote in both the House and Senate without input from the governor, one way Virginia's legislature is stronger than its executive. The [governor](253093.md) can make [recess appointments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recess_appointment), and when both branches are controlled by the same party, the assembly often confirms them. The judicial hierarchy starts with the [General District Courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_District_Court) and [Juvenile and Domestic Relations District Courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Juvenile_and_Domestic_Relations_District_Court), with the [Circuit Courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Circuit_Court) above them, then the [Court of Appeals of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Appeals_of_Virginia), and the [Supreme Court of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Virginia) on top.[^cite_note-328] The Supreme Court has seven justices who serve 12-year terms, with a [mandatory retirement age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_retirement_age) of 73; they select their own chief justice, who is informally limited to two four-year terms.[^cite_note-329] Virginia was the last state to guarantee an automatic right of [appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appeal) for all civil and criminal cases. Its Court of Appeals increased from 11 to 17 judges in 2021.[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331]

The [Code of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Code_of_Virginia) is the statutory law and consists of the codified legislation of the General Assembly. The largest [law enforcement agency in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_law_enforcement_agencies_in_Virginia) is the [Virginia State Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_State_Police), with 3,035 sworn and civilian members as of 2019.[^cite_note-332] The [Virginia Marine Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Marine_Police) were founded as the "Oyster Navy" in 1864 in response to [oyster bed poaching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oyster_pirate).[^cite_note-333] The [Virginia Capitol Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Division_of_Capitol_Police) protect the legislature and executive department, and are the oldest police department in the United States, dating to the guards who protected the colonial leadership.[^cite_note-334] The governor can also call upon the [Virginia National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_National_Guard), which consists of approximately [7,200 army soldiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Army_National_Guard), [1,200 airmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Air_National_Guard), [300 Defense Force members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Defense_Force), and 400 civilians.[^cite_note-335]

Between 1608 and 2021, when the [death penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_Virginia) was abolished, the state executed over 1,300 people, including [113 following the resumption of capital punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_executed_in_Virginia) in 1982.[^cite_note-336] Virginia's prison system incarcerates 30,936 people as of 2018, 53% of whom are Black,[^cite_note-337] and the state has the 16th-highest [rate of incarceration in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_incarceration_and_correctional_supervision_rate), at 422 per 100,000 residents.[^cite_note-338] Prisoner [parole](342305.md) was ended in 1995,[^cite_note-339] and Virginia's rate of [recidivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recidivism) of released felons who are re-convicted within three years and sentenced to a year or more is 23.1%, the lowest in the country as of 2019.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341] Virginia has the fourth lowest violent crime rate and 13th lowest property crime rate as of 2018.[^cite_note-342] Between 2008 and 2017, arrests for drug-related crimes rose 38%, with 71% of those related to [marijuana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannabis_in_Virginia),[^cite_note-343] which Virginia [decriminalized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decriminalization_of_non-medical_cannabis_in_the_United_States) in July 2020 and [legalized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legality_of_cannabis_by_U.S._jurisdiction) in July 2021.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345]

## Politics

Main article: [Politics of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Virginia)

People stroll in a wooded area decorated with American flags.

Mirroring Virginia's political transition, the annual [Shad Planking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shad_Planking) event in [Wakefield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wakefield,_Virginia) has evolved from a vestige of the [Byrd era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byrd_Organization) into a regular stop for many state campaigns.

[^cite_note-shad-346]

Over the past century, Virginia has shifted politically from being a largely rural, conservative, [Southern bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solid_South) member to a state that is more urbanized, pluralistic, and politically moderate, as both greater enfranchisement and demographic shifts have changed the electorate. Up until the 1970s, Virginia was a racially divided one-party state dominated by the [Byrd Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byrd_Organization).[^cite_note-347] They sought to stymie the political power of Northern Virginia, perpetuate [segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Segregation_in_the_United_States), and successfully restricted voter registration such that between 1905 and 1948 [voter turnout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_turnout) was regularly below ten percent.[^cite_note-atlantic-348][^cite_note-349]

Enforcement of federal civil rights legislation passed in the mid-1960s helped overturn the state's [Jim Crow laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws) that effectively [disenfranchised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disfranchisement) African Americans.[^cite_note-promiseprejudice-350] The [Voting Rights Act of 1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_Rights_Act_of_1965) made Virginia one of nine states that were required to receive federal approval for changes to voting laws, until the system for including states was [struck down in 2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_County_v._Holder).[^cite_note-351] The [Voting Rights Act of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_Rights_Act_of_Virginia) was passed in 2021, requiring preclearance from the [state Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_Virginia) for local election changes that could result in disenfranchisement, including closing or moving polling sites.[^cite_note-352] Though many Jim Crow provisions were removed in Virginia's 1971 constitution, a lifetime [ban on voting for felony convictions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felony_disenfranchisement_in_Virginia) was unchanged, and by 2016, up to 20 percent of African Americans in Virginia were disenfranchised because of prior felonies.[^cite_note-353] That year, Governor [Terry McAuliffe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terry_McAuliffe) ended the lifetime ban and individually restored voting rights to over 200,000 ex-felons.[^cite_note-atlantic-348] Virginia moved from being ranked as the second most difficult state to vote in 2016, to the 12th easiest in 2020.[^cite_note-354]

While urban and expanding suburban areas, including much of [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia), form the modern [Democratic Party](5043544.md) [base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Base_\(politics\)), rural southern and western areas moved to support the [Republican Party](32070.md) in response to its "[southern strategy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_strategy)" starting around 1970.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] Rural Democratic support has nevertheless persisted in union-influenced [Roanoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke,_Virginia), college towns such as [Charlottesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville,_Virginia) and [Blacksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacksburg,_Virginia), and the southeastern [Black Belt Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Belt_in_the_American_South).[^cite_note-357] African Americans are the most reliable bloc of Democratic voters,[^cite_note-promiseprejudice-350] but educational attainment and gender have also become strong indicators of political alignment, with the majority of women in Virginia supporting Democratic presidential candidates since 1980.[^cite_note-358] International immigration and domestic migration into Virginia have also increased the proportion of eligible voters born outside the state from 44% in 1980 to 55% in 2019.[^cite_note-359]

### State elections

See also: [Elections in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Virginia) and [Political party strength in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_party_strength_in_Virginia)

A map of Virginia showing the results of the 2025 Virginia House of Delegates election, with Republican districts in red and Democratic districts in blue, with heavier shading showing which changed parties.

[2025 Virginia House of Delegates election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Virginia_House_of_Delegates_election)

A map of Virginia showing the results of the 2023 Virginia Senate election, with Republican districts in red and Democratic districts in blue, with heavier shading showing which changed parties.

[2023 Virginia Senate election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Virginia_Senate_election)

&#x20; Republican hold    Democratic hold\
&#x20; Republican gain    Democratic gain

Because Virginia enacted their post-Civil-War [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Virginia) in 1870, state elections in Virginia occur in odd-numbered years, with executive department elections occurring in years following U.S. presidential elections and [State Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_Virginia) elections occurring in the years prior to presidential elections.[^cite_note-offyear-360] [House of Delegates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_House_of_Delegates) elections take place concurrent with each of those elections. National politics often play a role in state election outcomes, and Virginians have elected governors of the party opposite the U.S. president in 11 of the last 12 contests, with only [Terry McAuliffe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terry_McAuliffe) beating the trend [in 2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_elections,_2013).[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362]

The [2017 state elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_elections,_2017) resulted in Democrats holding the three executive offices, as lieutenant governor [Ralph Northam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ralph_Northam) won [the race for governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Virginia_gubernatorial_election). In concurrent [House of Delegates elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_House_of_Delegates_elections,_2017), Democrats flipped 15 of the Republicans' previous 16-seat majority.[^cite_note-363] Control of the House came down to a tied election in the [94th district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia's_94th_House_of_Delegates_district), which the Republican won by a drawing of lots, giving the party a slim 51–49 majority in the [2018–19 legislative sessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/160th_Virginia_General_Assembly).[^cite_note-364] Adjusted districts were used in the [2019 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_elections,_2019), when Democrats won full control of the General Assembly, despite [a political crisis earlier that year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Virginia_political_crisis).[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366]

In 2021, [Glenn Youngkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Youngkin) became the first Republican to [win the governor's race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Virginia_gubernatorial_election) since 2009,[^cite_note-367] with his party also winning the races for [lieutenant governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Virginia_lieutenant_gubernatorial_election) and [attorney general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Virginia_Attorney_General_election) and gaining [seven seats in the House of Delegates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Virginia_House_of_Delegates_election).[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] Two years later, new legislative maps drawn by [special masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_master) appointed by [the state supreme court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Virginia) led to nine retirements in the state senate and to twenty-five House delegates not seeking re-election. In [those elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Virginia_elections), Democrats claimed a slim majority of one seat in both the Senate and the House.[^cite_note-370] In 2025, Democrat [Abigail Spanberger](58910530.md) won the [2025 Virginia gubernatorial election](69194251.md), becoming the first female governor of Virginia.[^cite_note-Virginia2025-371]

### Federal elections

See also: [United States presidential elections in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_elections_in_Virginia)



[2024 U.S. presidential election results](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election_in_Virginia) by county in Virginia&#x20;

&#x20; Democratic

&#x20; Republican

Two older men in suits address a group of teenagers assembled on the steps of the U.S. Capitol

[U.S. Senators](24909346.md) [Mark Warner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Warner) and [Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine), both former [governors](253093.md), meet with students on the steps of the [U.S. Capitol](31979.md).

Though Virginia was considered a "[swing state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state)" in the [2008 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_United_States_presidential_election),[^cite_note-purple-372] Virginia's 13 [electoral votes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_College_\(United_States\)) were carried in that election and the four since then by Democratic candidates, suggesting the state has shifted to being reliably Democratic in presidential elections. Virginia was the only former Confederate state to vote for the Democrats in the [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election_in_Virginia) and [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election_in_Virginia) presidential elections. Virginia had previously voted for Republican presidential candidates in 13 out of 14 [presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_election) from 1952 to 2004, including ten in a row from 1968 to 2004.[^cite_note-breeze-373] Virginia currently holds its presidential [open primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_primaries_in_the_United_States) election on [Super Tuesday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Tuesday), the same day as 14 other states, with [the most recent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Virginia_Republican_presidential_primary) held on March 5, 2024.[^cite_note-374]

[Virginia's two U.S. senators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_senators_from_Virginia) are in [classes 1 and 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classes_of_United_States_senators). Virginia has had 11 [U.S. House of Representatives seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia's_congressional_delegations) since 1993, and control of the majority has flipped four times since then, often as part of "[wave elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wave_elections_in_the_United_States)". Currently, six representatives are Democrats and five are Republicans.[^cite_note-375]

### Gerrymandering

Main article: [Redistricting in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redistricting_in_Virginia)

Accusations of manipulating legislative districts to improve a party or candidates' chances of winning elections in Virginia go back to the [first U.S. Congress election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_United_States_Congress) in 1789, when [Patrick Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Henry) and his [Anti-Federalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Federalism) allies allegedly drew the boundaries of [Virginia's 5th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia's_5th_congressional_district) in an unsuccessful attempt to keep [James Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Madison) out of the [U.S. House of Representatives](19468510.md).[^cite_note-376] During the [Jim Crow era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_era), the [Byrd Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byrd_Organization) used [malapportionment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malapportionment) to manipulate what areas were over-represented in the [General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_Assembly) and the U.S. Congress until ordered to end the practice by the 1964 [U.S. Supreme Court](31737.md) decision in *[Davis v. Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_v._Mann)* and the 1965 [Virginia Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Supreme_Court) decision in *Wilkins v. Davis* respectively.[^cite_note-377]

After the [2010 U.S. census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_U.S._census), under Governor [Bob McDonnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_McDonnell) and a Republican controlled General Assembly, Virginia enacted congressional and legislative maps favorable to that party. This led to Virginia being ranked in 2018 as having the most [gerrymandered](42223515.md) U.S. state legislature, as Republicans controlled the House with only 44.5% of the total vote.[^cite_note-378] The maps were, however, challenged in court, and in 2014, Federal judges ruled Virginia's U.S. House map to be unconstitutionally drawn to discriminate against African Americans.[^cite_note-379] Then in 2019, 11 state house district lines were [ordered to be redrawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bethune-Hill_v._Virginia_State_Bd._of_Elections) for the same reason.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381]

Voters in 2020 then [passed a referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Virginia_Question_1) to give [control of drawing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redistricting_in_Virginia) both state and congressional districts to a commission of eight citizens and four legislators from each of the two major parties, rather than the legislature.[^cite_note-382] This commission, however, failed to come to a compromise, requiring the [Supreme Court of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Virginia) to appoint [special masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_master) to design maps. As part of a larger [mid-cycle redistricting effort across the country](81439539.md), voters approved [another referendum in 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Virginia_redistricting_amendment) to allow the Democratic Party majority in the [General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_Assembly) to redraw U.S. House districts in way that could favor their party during [the 2026](78311563.md) and two subsequent congressional election cycles,[^cite_note-383] but this referendum was subsequently declared null and void by the Virginia Supreme Court.[^cite_note-384]

## Education

Main article: [Education in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Virginia)

Five middle school students work together at a table using a soldering iron

Middle school students in [Albemarle County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albemarle_County,_Virginia) participate in an engineering program in partnership with the [Smithsonian Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian_Institution).

Virginia's educational system consistently ranks in the top five states on the [U.S. Department of Education's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Education) [National Assessment of Educational Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assessment_of_Educational_Progress), with Virginia students outperforming the average in all subject areas and grade levels tested.[^cite_note-385] Virginia's K–7 schools had a [student–teacher ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Student%E2%80%93teacher_ratio) of 12.41\:1 as of the 2022–23 school year, and 12.52\:1 for grades 8–12.[^cite_note-386] All school divisions must adhere to educational standards set forth by the [Virginia Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Department_of_Education), which maintains an assessment and accreditation regime known as the [Standards of Learning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standards_of_Learning).[^cite_note-387]

Public [K–12 schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%E2%80%9312_\(education\)) in Virginia are generally operated by the counties and cities, and not by the state. As of the 2023–24 academic year, 1,261,962 students were enrolled in 2,254 local and regional schools in the Commonwealth, including 56 career and technical schools and 290 alternative and special education centers across 126 [school divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_school_divisions_in_Virginia). Besides the general [public schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_school_\(government-funded\)) in Virginia, there are [Governor's Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor's_Schools_\(Virginia\)) and selective [magnet schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnet_school). The Governor's Schools are a collection of 52 regional high schools and summer programs intended for gifted students,[^cite_note-doedata-388][^cite_note-389] and include the [Thomas Jefferson High School for Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson_High_School_for_Science_and_Technology), the top-rated high school in the country in 2022.[^cite_note-390] The Virginia Council for Private Education oversees the regulation of 483 state accredited private schools.[^cite_note-391] An additional 53,680 students receive homeschooling.[^cite_note-392]

In 2022, 92.1% of high school students graduated on-time after four years,[^cite_note-grad2022-393] and 91.3% of adults over the age 25 had their high school diploma.[^cite_note-QuickFacts-3] Virginia has one of the smaller racial gaps in graduation rates among U.S. states,[^cite_note-394] with 91.7% of Black students graduating on time. White non-Hispanic students, by comparison, had a graduation rate of 95.6%, while Asian students saw the highest numbers, with 97.8%. Hispanic students had the highest [dropout rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_school_dropouts_in_the_United_States), at 12.5% as of 2025, with high rates being correlated with students listed as [English learners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_as_a_second_or_foreign_language).[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-grad2022-393] Despite ending [school segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_segregation_in_the_United_States) in the 1960s, seven percent of Virginia's public schools were rated as "intensely segregated" by [The Civil Rights Project at UCLA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Civil_Rights_Project/Proyecto_Derechos_Civiles) in 2019, and the number has risen since 1989, when only three percent were.[^cite_note-396] Virginia has comparatively large public school districts, typically comprising entire counties or cities, and while this helps mitigate funding gaps seen in other states, districts that serve the most students of color received \$722 less per student than those severing the fewest, as of 2022.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398] Elementary schools, with Virginia's smallest districts, were found to be more segregated than state middle or high schools by a 2019 VCU study.[^cite_note-399]

### Colleges and universities

See also: [List of colleges and universities in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_Virginia)



The [University of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Virginia) guarantees full tuition scholarships to all in-state Virginia students with family incomes of \$80,000 or less.

[^cite_note-400]

As of 2020, Virginia has the [eighth-highest percent of residents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_educational_attainment) with bachelor's degrees or higher, with 41.5%.[^cite_note-QuickFacts-3] The [Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Education) recognizes 163 [colleges and universities in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_Virginia).[^cite_note-nces-401] In the 2022 *[U.S. News & World Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report)* ranking of national public universities, the [University of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Virginia) is ranked 3rd, the [College of William and Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_William_and_Mary) is 13th, [Virginia Tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Tech) is 23rd, [George Mason University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Mason_University) is 65th, [James Madison University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Madison_University) is 72nd, and [Virginia Commonwealth University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Commonwealth_University) is 83rd.[^cite_note-402] There are 119 private institutions in the state, including [Washington and Lee University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_and_Lee_University) and the [University of Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Richmond), which are ranked as the country's 11th and 18th best liberal arts colleges respectively.[^cite_note-nces-401][^cite_note-403]
