Virginia Tech and [Virginia State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_State_University) are the state's [land-grant universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land-grant_university), and Virginia State is one of its five [historically Black colleges and universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historically_Black_colleges_and_universities).[^cite_note-404] The [Virginia Military Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Military_Institute) is the oldest state [military college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_academy).[^cite_note-405] Virginia also operates [23 community colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Community_College_System) on 40 campuses which enrolled 199,926 degree-seeking students during the 2021–2022 school year.[^cite_note-406] In 2021, the state made community college free for most low- and middle-income students.[^cite_note-407] George Mason University had the largest on-campus enrollment at 40,390 students as of 2023,[^cite_note-408] though the private [Liberty University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_University) had the largest total enrollment in the state, with 115,000 online and 15,800 on-campus students in [Lynchburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynchburg,_Virginia) as of 2022.[^cite_note-409]

## Health

Two medical professionals, one holding a clipboard, in blue scrubs and facemasks stand outside the window of a dark blue car parked in front of a brick building.

Patients are screened for [COVID-19](63030231.md) outside [Naval Medical Center Portsmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Medical_Center_Portsmouth), the [Navy](20518076.md)'s oldest continuously operating hospital.

[^cite_note-410]

Virginia was ranked best for its physical environment in the 2024 United Health Foundation's Health Rankings, but 15th for its overall health outcomes and only 23rd for residents' healthy behaviors. Among U.S. states, Virginia has the 20th-lowest rate of premature deaths, with 8,146 per 100,000,[^cite_note-ahr-411] and an [infant mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) rate of 5.61 per 1,000 live births.[^cite_note-412] The rate of uninsured Virginians dropped to 6.4% in 2024, following an expansion of [Medicare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicare_\(United_States\)) in 2019.[^cite_note-ahr-411] [Falls Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falls_Church,_Virginia) and [Loudoun County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudoun_County,_Virginia) were both ranked in the top ten healthiest communities in 2020 by *U.S. News & World Report*.[^cite_note-413]

With high rates of heart disease and diabetes, African Americans in Virginia have an average life expectancy over four years below the state's average, and over ten less than Asian-American and Hispanic Virginians,[^cite_note-414] and were disproportionately affected by the [coronavirus pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Virginia).[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] African-American mothers are also three times more likely to [die while giving birth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maternal_mortality_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-417] Mortality rates among White middle-class Virginians have also been rising, with drug overdose, alcohol poisoning, and suicide as leading causes.[^cite_note-418] Suicides in the state increased over 14% between 2009 and 2023, while deaths from drug overdoses more than doubled.[^cite_note-ahr-411] Virginia has a ratio of 274.3 [primary care physicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_care_physician) per 10,000 residents and only 273.1 mental health providers per that number, both 14th worst nationwide.[^cite_note-ahr-411] A December 2023 report by the [General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_General_Assembly) found that all nine public mental health care facilities were over 95% full, causing overcrowding and delays in admissions.[^cite_note-419]

Weight is an issue for many Virginians: 32.2% of adults and 14.9% of 10- to 17-year-olds are obese as of 2021,[^cite_note-420] 35% of adults are overweight, and 23.3% do not exercise regularly.[^cite_note-421] Smoking in bars and restaurants was banned in January 2010,[^cite_note-422] and the percent of tobacco smokers in the state has declined from 19% in that year to 12.1% in 2023, but an additional 7.7% use [e-cigarettes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-cigarette). The percentage of adults who receive annual immunizations is above average, as 48.1% get their yearly flu vaccination.[^cite_note-ahr-411] In 2008, Virginia became the first U.S. state to mandate the [HPV vaccine](3304705.md) for girls for school attendance,[^cite_note-423] and 62.9% of adolescents have the vaccine as of 2024.[^cite_note-ahr-411]

The [Virginia Board of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Board_of_Health) regulates healthcare facilities. There are 88 [hospitals in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_hospitals_in_Virginia) with a combined 17,024 hospital beds as of 2023. The largest in both Virginia and the [Washington metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_metropolitan_area) is [Inova Fairfax Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inova_Fairfax_Hospital), which serves over 55,000 patients annually.[^cite_note-424] [VCU Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VCU_Medical_Center), where a new 16-story [children's hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_hospital) was opened in 2023, is highly ranked for [pediatrics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pediatrics),[^cite_note-425] while [UVA Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Virginia_Health_System) is highly ranked for its cancer care,[^cite_note-426] and the state numbers in the top ten for annual [cancer screenings](22848246.md).[^cite_note-ahr-411] [Sentara Norfolk General Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sentara_Norfolk_General_Hospital), a teaching institution of [Eastern Virginia Medical School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Virginia_Medical_School), was the site of the [first successful](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Jordan_Carr) U.S. [in-vitro fertilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-vitro_fertilization) program, and around 2.5% of births in the state are due to IVF.[^cite_note-427]

## Media

See also: [List of newspapers in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Virginia), [List of radio stations in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_radio_stations_in_Virginia), and [List of television stations in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_television_stations_in_Virginia)

A man in a military uniform is being interviewed by a woman holding a microphone and a man holding a television camera.

Journalists from [WAVY-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WAVY-TV) reporting on a coastal storm damage reduction project in [Norfolk](57898.md)

The [Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads) area is the 44th-largest [media market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_market) in the United States as ranked by [Nielsen Media Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nielsen_Media_Research), while the [Richmond-Petersburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond-Petersburg) area is 56th and [Roanoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke,_Virginia)-[Lynchburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynchburg,_Virginia) is 71st as of 2022. Northern Virginia is part of the much larger Washington, D.C. media market, which is the country's ninth-largest.[^cite_note-428]

There are 36 [television stations in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_television_stations_in_Virginia), representing each major [U.S. network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_over-the-air_television_networks), part of 42 stations which serve Virginia viewers including those broadcasting from neighboring jurisdictions.[^cite_note-429] There are 595 [FCC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Communications_Commission)-licensed FM [radio stations broadcast in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_radio_stations_in_Virginia) and 239 AM stations as of 2020.[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431] The nationally available [Public Broadcasting Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Broadcasting_Service) (PBS) is headquartered in [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia). Independent PBS affiliates exist throughout Virginia, and the Arlington [PBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS) member station [WETA-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WETA-TV) produces programs such as the *[PBS NewsHour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS_NewsHour)* and *[Washington Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Week)*.

The most circulated [native newspapers in the Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Virginia) are [Norfolk's](57898.md) *[The Virginian-Pilot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Virginian-Pilot)* with around 132,000 subscribers,[^cite_note-432] the *[Richmond Times-Dispatch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Times-Dispatch)* with 86,219,[^cite_note-433] and *[The Roanoke Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roanoke_Times)* as of 2018.[^cite_note-434] *[USA Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today)*, which is headquartered in [McLean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLean,_Virginia), has seen its daily subscription number decline significantly from over 500,000 in 2019 to just over 180,000 in 2021, but is still the third-most circulated paper nationwide.[^cite_note-435] *USA Today* is the flagship publication of [Gannett, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gannett), which merged with [GateHouse Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GateHouse_Media) in 2019, and operates over 100 local newspapers nationwide.[^cite_note-436] In Northern Virginia, *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* is the dominant newspaper and provides local coverage for the region.[^cite_note-437] *[Politico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politico_\(newspaper\))* and *[Axios](52436164.md)*, which both cover national politics, have their headquarters in [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia).[^cite_note-438]

## Transportation

Main article: [Transportation in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Virginia)

A train station built over a busy intersection in front of several skyscrapers at sunset.

The [Silver Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_Line_\(Washington_Metro\)) extension of the [Washington Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Metro) system opened in [Tysons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tysons,_Virginia) in 2014.

Because of the 1932 [Byrd Road Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byrd_Road_Act), the state government controls most of Virginia's roads, instead of a local county authority as is usual in other states.[^cite_note-439] As of 2018, the [Virginia Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Department_of_Transportation) (VDOT) owns and operates 57,867 miles (93,128 km) of the total 70,105 miles (112,823 km) of roads in the state, making it the third-largest state highway system.[^cite_note-vdot-440]

Traffic on Virginia's roads is among the worst in the nation according to the 2019 American Community Survey. The average commute time of 28.7 minutes is the eighth-longest among U.S. states, and the Washington Metropolitan Area, which includes [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia), has the second-worst rate of traffic congestion among U.S. cities.[^cite_note-441] About 68.4% of workers in Virginia reported driving alone to work in 2024, the 15th-lowest percent in the U.S.,[^cite_note-ahr-411] while 8.2% reported carpooling,[^cite_note-acs_transit-442] and Virginia hit [peak car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peak_car) usage before the year 2000, making it one of the first such states.[^cite_note-443]

### Mass transit and ports

About 3.4% of Virginians commute on public transit,[^cite_note-acs_transit-442] and there were over 171.9 million public transit trips in Virginia in 2019, over 62% of which were done on the [Washington Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Metro) transit system, which serves [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia) and [Alexandria](18940583.md), and extends into [Loudoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudoun_County,_Virginia) and [Fairfax Counties](91327.md).[^cite_note-wmata-444] Commuter buses include the [Fairfax Connector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairfax_Connector), [FRED](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fredericksburg_Regional_Transit) buses in Fredericksburg, and [OmniRide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potomac_and_Rappahannock_Transportation_Commission) in [Prince William County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_William_County,_Virginia),[^cite_note-445] while the state-run [Virginia Breeze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Breeze) buses run four inter-city routes from [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) to [Bristol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol,_Virginia), [Blacksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacksburg,_Virginia), [Martinsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinsville,_Virginia), and [Danville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danville,_Virginia).[^cite_note-446] VDOT operates several free ferries throughout Virginia, the most notable being the [Jamestown Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown_Ferry) which connects [Jamestown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown,_Virginia) to [Scotland Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland,_Virginia) across the [James River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_River).[^cite_note-447]

Virginia has [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) passenger rail service along several corridors, and [Virginia Railway Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Railway_Express) (VRE) maintains two commuter lines into Washington, D.C. from [Fredericksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fredericksburg,_Virginia) and [Manassas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manassas,_Virginia). VRE experienced a dramatic decline in ridership due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Virginia), with daily ridership dropping from over 18,000 in 2019 to 6,864 in February 2024.[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449] Amtrak routes in Virginia have however passed their pre-pandemic levels and served 123,658 passengers in March 2024.[^cite_note-450] [Norfolk](57898.md) operates a light rail system called [The Tide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tide_\(light_rail_network\)), servicing about 2,300 people per day.[^cite_note-451] Major freight railroads in Virginia include [Norfolk Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Southern) and [CSX Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSX_Transportation), and in 2021 the state finalized a deal to purchase 223 miles (359 km) of track and over 350 miles (560 km) of right of way from CSX for future passenger rail service.[^cite_note-452]

Virginia has five major airports: [Dulles International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dulles_International_Airport) and [Reagan Washington National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_Washington_National_Airport) in [Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Virginia), both of which handle over 20 million passengers a year, [Richmond International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_International_Airport) southeast of the state capital, [Newport News/Williamsburg International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport_News/Williamsburg_International_Airport), and [Norfolk International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_International_Airport). Several other airports offer limited commercial passenger service, and sixty-six public airports serve the state's aviation needs.[^cite_note-453] The [Virginia Port Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Port_Authority)'s main seaports are those in Hampton Roads, which carried 61,505,700 [short tons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Short_ton) (55,797,000 [t](31185.md)) of total cargo in 2021, the sixth most of United States ports.[^cite_note-454] The [Eastern Shore of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Shore_of_Virginia) is the site of [Wallops Flight Facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallops_Flight_Facility), a rocket launch center owned by [NASA](18426568.md), and the [Mid-Atlantic Regional Spaceport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Atlantic_Regional_Spaceport), a commercial spaceport.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodwin2012305-455][^cite_note-456] [Space tourism](67896.md) is also offered through [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna,_Virginia)-based [Space Adventures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Adventures).[^cite_note-457]

## Sports

See also: [Sports teams in Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_teams_in_Virginia)

A large crowd of runners in brightly colored shirts race down a wide street bordered by autumnal trees.

The annual [Monument Avenue 10K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monument_Avenue_10K) in [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia), one of the ten largest timed long-distance running races in the U.S.

[^cite_note-458]

Virginia is the most populous U.S. state without a [major professional sports league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada) franchise. The reasons for this include the lack of any dominant city or market within the state and the proximity of [teams in Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Washington,_D.C.), [Baltimore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Baltimore), [Charlotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Charlotte,_North_Carolina), and [Raleigh](57845.md), as well as a reluctance to publicly finance stadiums.[^cite_note-459] A proposed [\$220 million NBA arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach_Arena) in [Virginia Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Beach) lost the support of the city council there in 2017,[^cite_note-460] while a 2023 proposal to move the NBA's [Washington Wizards](72858.md) and the NHL's [Washington Capitals](73125.md) to [Alexandria](18940583.md) was canceled after opposition in the [Virginia Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Senate).[^cite_note-461]

Five [minor league baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_league_baseball) and two mid-level hockey teams play in Virginia. Norfolk is host to two: The [Triple-A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple-A_\(baseball\)) [Norfolk Tides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Tides) and the [ECHL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ECHL)'s [Norfolk Admirals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Admirals_\(ECHL\)). The [Double-A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double-A_\(baseball\)) [Richmond Flying Squirrels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Flying_Squirrels) began playing at [The Diamond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Diamond_\(Richmond,_Virginia\)) in 2010,[^cite_note-462] while the [Fredericksburg Nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fredericksburg_Nationals), [Lynchburg Hillcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynchburg_Hillcats), and [Salem RidgeYaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_RidgeYaks) play in the [Carolina League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolina_League).[^cite_note-463] [Loudoun United FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudoun_United_FC), the reserve team of [D.C. United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D.C._United), debuted in the [USL Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_Championship) in 2019,[^cite_note-auto-464] while the [Richmond Kickers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Kickers) of the [USL League One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_League_One) have operated since 1993 and are the only team in their league to win both the league championship and the [U.S. Open Cup](253156.md) in the same year.[^cite_note-465] The training facilities for both the [Washington Commanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Commanders) and [Washington Spirit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Spirit) are in [Loudoun County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudoun_County,_Virginia),[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467] while the [Washington Capitals](73125.md) practice at [MedStar Capitals Iceplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MedStar_Capitals_Iceplex) in [Ballston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballston,_Virginia).[^cite_note-468]

[Hampton Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Roads) has produced several Olympic gold medalists, including [Gabby Douglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabby_Douglas), the first African American to win [gymnastics individual all-around](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gymnastics_at_the_2012_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Women's_artistic_individual_all-around) gold,[^cite_note-469] and [LaShawn Merritt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LaShawn_Merritt), [Francena McCorory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francena_McCorory), and [Michael Cherry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Cherry_\(athlete\)), who have all won gold in the [4 × 400 meters relay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_%C3%97_400_meters_relay).[^cite_note-470] [Noah Lyles](51135146.md), winner of the [100 meter dash at the 2024 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletics_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_100_metres), grew up in [Alexandria](18940583.md).[^cite_note-471] Major long-distance races in the state include the [Richmond Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Marathon), the [Blue Ridge Marathon on the Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge_Marathon_on_the_Parkway), and the [Monument Avenue 10K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monument_Avenue_10K). Virginia's professional caliber golf courses include [Kingsmill Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingsmill_Resort) outside [Williamsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williamsburg,_Virginia), which hosts [an LPGA Tour tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pure_Silk_Championship) in May, and the [Country Club of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Club_of_Virginia) outside [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia), which hosts [a charity classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_Energy_Charity_Classic) on the [PGA Tour Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour_Champions) in October. Notable PGA Tour winners from Virginia include [Sam Snead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Snead) and [Curtis Strange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curtis_Strange). [NASCAR](21114.md) currently schedules [Cup Series races](56571945.md) on two tracks in Virginia: [Martinsville Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinsville_Speedway) and [Richmond Raceway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Raceway). Notable drivers from Virginia in the series have included [Jeff Burton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Burton), [Ward Burton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ward_Burton), [Denny Hamlin](2148196.md), [Wendell Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wendell_Scott) and [Curtis Turner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curtis_Turner).[^cite_note-472]

### College sports

A college basketball player dressed in white with orange and blue bordering prepares to shoot a free throw.

[Mike Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Scott_\(basketball,_born_1988\)) and [Joe Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Harris_\(basketball\)) of the [Virginia Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Cavaliers_men's_basketball) battle [Cadarian Raines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadarian_Raines) of the [Virginia Tech Hokies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Tech_Hokies_men's_basketball) for a rebound in a [college basketball](768856.md) game at [Cassell Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassell_Coliseum) in [Blacksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacksburg,_Virginia).

Several of Virginia's collegiate sports programs have attracted strong followings, with a 2015 poll showing that 34% of Virginians were fans of the [Virginia Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Cavaliers) and 28% were fans of the [rival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia-Virginia_Tech_rivalry) [Virginia Tech Hokies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Tech_Hokies), making both more popular than the surveyed regional professional teams.[^cite_note-PPP-473] The men's and women's [college basketball](768856.md) programs of the [Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Cavaliers_men's_basketball), [VCU Rams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VCU_Rams_men's_basketball), and [Old Dominion Monarchs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Dominion_Monarchs_basketball) have combined for 66 regular season conference championships and 49 conference tournament championships between them as of 2023. The [Hokies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Tech_Hokies_football) football team sustained a 27-year bowl streak between 1993 and 2019; [James Madison Dukes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Madison_Dukes_football) football won [FCS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_I_Football_Championship_Subdivision) NCAA Championships in both 2004 and 2016.[^cite_note-474] The overall UVA men's athletics programs won the national [Capital One Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_One_Cup_\(college_sports\)) in [both 2015 and 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_One_Cup_\(college_sports\)#Champions), and led the [Atlantic Coast Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Coast_Conference) in [NCAA championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Coast_Conference#NCAA_team_championships).[^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476]

[Fourteen universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_college_athletic_programs_in_Virginia#Division_I) in total compete in [NCAA Division I](669931.md), with multiple programs each in the Atlantic Coast Conference, [Atlantic 10 Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_10_Conference), [Big South Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_South_Conference), and [Coastal Athletic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_Athletic_Association). Three [historically Black schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historically_black_colleges_and_universities) compete in the Division II [Central Intercollegiate Athletic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Intercollegiate_Athletic_Association), and two others ([Hampton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Pirates_and_Lady_Pirates) and [Norfolk State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_State_Spartans)) compete in Division I. Several smaller schools compete in the [Old Dominion Athletic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Dominion_Athletic_Conference) and the [USA South Athletic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_South_Athletic_Conference) of NCAA Division III. The NCAA currently holds its [Division III championships in football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_III_national_football_championship), men's basketball, volleyball, and softball in [Salem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem,_Virginia).[^cite_note-477] State appropriated funds are not allowed to be used for either operational or capital expenses for intercollegiate athletics.[^cite_note-478]

### High school sports

Virginia is also home to several of the nation's top high school [basketball](3921.md) programs, including [Paul VI Catholic High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_VI_Catholic_High_School) and [Oak Hill Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oak_Hill_Academy_\(Mouth_of_Wilson,_Virginia\)), the latter of which has won nine national championships.[^cite_note-479] In the 2022–2023 school year, 176,623 high school students participated in 14 girls sports and 13 boys sports managed by the [Virginia High School League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_High_School_League), with the most popular sports being [football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_school_football), outdoor track and cross country, [soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_school_soccer), basketball, baseball and softball, and volleyball.[^cite_note-480] Outside of the high school system, 145 youth soccer clubs operate in the Virginia Youth Soccer Association, under the [USYS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Youth_Soccer_Association) system, as of 2024.[^cite_note-481]

## State symbols

Main article: [List of Virginia state symbols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Virginia_state_symbols)

A large rectangular metal sign, mostly black, with the words "Welcome To Virginia" and "Virginia is for lovers" with a red heart symbol on the left stands to the right of a rural road through green hills.

The state slogan, "[Virginia Is for Lovers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Is_for_Lovers)", has been used since 1969 and is featured on state welcome signs.

[^cite_note-482]

Virginia has several nicknames, the oldest of which is the "Old Dominion". King [Charles II of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_II_of_England) first referred to "our auntient Collonie of Virginia" one of "our own Dominions" in 1662 or 1663, perhaps choosing this language because Virginia was home to many of his supporters during the [English Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Civil_War).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerkeleyBillingsKimberly2007184–185-483][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELibrary_of_Virginia199488-484] These supporters were called [Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cavalier), and the nickname "The Cavalier State" was popularized after the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWelch20061–3-485] Virginia has also been called the "Mother of Presidents", as eight Virginians have served as [President of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lists_of_United_States_Presidents_by_place_of_birth#By_state_of_birth), including four of the first five.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodwin201211–13-486]

The state's motto, *[Sic Semper Tyrannis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic_semper_tyrannis)*, translates from [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin) as "Thus Always to Tyrants", and is used on the state seal, which is then used on the flag.[^cite_note-sic-1] While the seal was designed in 1776, and the flag was first used in the 1830s, both were made official in 1930.[^cite_note-487] In 1940, "[Carry Me Back to Old Virginny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carry_Me_Back_to_Old_Virginny)" was named the [state song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_songs), but it was retired in 1997 due to its nostalgic references to slavery. In March 2015, Virginia's government named "[Our Great Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Great_Virginia)", which uses the tune of "[Oh Shenandoah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oh_Shenandoah)", as the traditional state song and "[Sweet Virginia Breeze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_Virginia_Breeze)" as the popular state song.[^cite_note-488]

- [Fossil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_fossils): *[Chesapecten jeffersonius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chesapecten_jeffersonius)*

- [Insect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_insects): [Tiger swallowtail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papilio_glaucus)

- [Mammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_mammals): [Virginia big-eared bat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_big-eared_bat)

- [Motto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_mottos): [Sic Semper Tyrannis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic_semper_tyrannis)
